Nvidia boss Jensen Huang resigns amid scandal surrounding two alleged affairs, interim CEO appointed as search for sexy leather jacket man's successor begins

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Guess his desperately initiated sway scheme failed, then.

Nvidia have ripped off the wax strip and are looking for a new CEO. Jensen Huang, he of the reported extramarital affairs with both a suspected Chinese spy and one of his executive underlings at Nvidia has removed his bottom from the great throne of black and green graphics cards.

"Jensen Huang has resigned as President, Chief Executive Officer, and a member of the Board, effective immediately," Nvidia declared in a press release announcing the "CEO transition" they're embarking on. Stepping up to replace Huang for the immediate future is current Nvidia executive vice president and chief financial officer Colette Kress, who is becoming interim CEO on top of her established number-crunching duties.

One would assume she's quickly calculated just how much extra work Jensen's alleged inability to not get amorous with women who aren't his wife will add to her plate until a permanent successor is found. Speaking of that, Nvidia wrote that they "will consider internal and external candidates" in their scanning for someone to step into Huang's leather jacket. Actually, Nvidia folks, maybe don't let the new person wear the leather jacket, lest people potentially find them dangerously attractive. Failing that, maybe a pair of padlocked underpants are in order, adorned with flashing green lights to ensure brand recognition in trouserless scenarios.

The company's board of directors have gone ahead and appointed Harvey Jones as their first ever chairperson, one would assume to help convince money people that they've got strong enough leadership in place until their new permanent boss is found.

"Nvidia’s strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years," interim CEO Kress said "We will continue advancing the work that has made Nvidia a leader in accelerated computing and AI, and execute on the opportunities ahead. Our focus remains on serving our customers, partners, developers and shareholders." Oh, come on, think how many people would cheer if you said you fancied pivoting even a tiny bit of attention away from AI and back to consumer GPUs.

"Jensen’s contributions to Nvidia are profound," the company's release concluded. "Nvidia is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. The Board and the company thank Jensen for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry."

Goodbye, leather jacket man who was allegedly too sexy for his own good, despite being a nerd who stands on stages waving hardware about. Odds are we'll see you again, possibly in the company of Elon Musk, because execs can never be allowed to be out of lucrative work for more than five minutes.

The Pokémon TCG's Delta Reign expansion has only been out since Nov. 6, but we've already seen the Mega Evolution expansion’s six-pack Booster Bundle get cheaper than ever on Amazon — just in time for Black Friday.

As of Nov. 12, the Pokémon TCG Delta Reign Booster Bundle can be bought for as low as $49.94 at Amazon, with $8.56 knocked off its typical $58.50 price. With 15% off, the cost of each of its six booster packs comes to $8.32. That's a sizeable drop from the $57.95 price we covered on launch day. Alan Shearer hails Arsenal star as the early ‘player of the season’ in the Premier League

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Alan Shearer has hailed Arsenal summer signing Ezri Konsa as the early ‘player of the season’ in the Premier League.

Konsa completed a £55m move to Arsenal over the summer after ending his seven-year spell at Aston Villa.

The England defender won the Europa League at Villa Park but has bigger ambitions with reigning Premier League champions and Champions League finalists Arsenal.

Konsa has missed just one game for Arsenal so far this season, helping Mikel Arteta’s side open up a five-point gap at the top of the Premier League and enjoy an impressive start to their European campaign.

The 29-year-old was signed partly due to the absence of William Saliba, who returned from last summer’s World Cup with a serious back injury.

Read exclusive analysis of the biggest stories in football with Metro's weekly football newsletter In The Mixer. Sign up now for free.

But Konsa’s form has led several pundits to question whether Saliba, widely regarded as one of the best defenders in the world, will even get straight back into the Arsenal team.

‘I think the player of the season up to now has been Ezri Konsa,’ Premier League record goalscorer and former England striker Shearer told Betfair.

‘I just think when you look at the performances of Arsenal defensively, and look at his performances defensively, we haven’t even really mentioned them missing William Saliba.

‘The only time we’ve mentioned him is in the last two weeks and that’s because everyone is thinking how is he going to get back in because Konsa has been that good.’

Use AI to go deeper into the stories you care about – powered by Metro and trusted publications.

Earlier this week Manchester United legend Rio Ferdinand said Konsa’s brilliant start at Arsenal had created a ‘problem’ for Arteta.

To view this video please enable JavaScript, and consider upgrading to a web browser that supports HTML5 video

‘Mikel Arteta has got a problem, he’s got a huge problem now,’ Ferdinand said on his YouTube channel.

‘What is he going to do when William Saliba is fit? Ezri Konsa has come into that back-four and he looks like he’s been there for ten years.

‘He looks like an immovable object in that team. That’s what it feels like because he’s just slid in there and taken that place. He looks dominant, aggressive and accustomed to wearing the Arsenal shirt.

‘He looks like he belongs there. But let’s not forget about Saliba and what he was doing when he was in the team. It’s a problem but it’s a problem that I hope Mikel Arteta will enjoy because he’s got two top centre-backs vying to be in the team.

‘It’s a good position to be in. I think we will see Konsa sometimes playing right-back.’

Arsenal’s nearest challengers going into the November international break is Brighton, who have won seven of their ten league games to climb to second in the Premier League.

‘Brighton could be Arsenal’s main challenge to the title this season,’ Shearer added.

‘I think Liverpool are in transition. Chelsea, same thing. They still have plenty of work to do with those football clubs.

‘Brighton have been incredible. The football that they’re playing, I mean, in Europe too, whether that will hinder them the longer the season goes on I don’t know, but they’re managing really well at the minute.

‘I don’t think they’re going to go away. I think there’ll be around for the majority of the season.’

Arsenal’s first game back following the international break is a potentially tricky trip to St James’ Park to face Newcastle United.

Spain will hope to maintain their 100 winning per cent record in the 2026/27 Nations League when they face the Czech Republic this evening.

Spain sit top of Group A3 courtesy of their 3-2 win against England at Wembley at the start of their Nations League campaign, enough to place La Roja three points above the Three Lions with two games remaining.

If Spain and England record expected wins against the Czech Republic and Croatia, respectively, then Sunday’s clash between the two teams will decide who enjoys seeded status in the draw for the quarter finals.

The hosts are led by Spaniard Santi Denia, who became the first foreigner to manage the Czech Republic after his appointment in the summer.

Denia’s side were unable to put up much of a fight in the reverse fixture, falling to a 3-1 defeat in Oviedo and seeing Ladislav Krejci dismissed five minutes from time.

Without a win in their four games in the Nations League so far this campaign, the Czechs need to find points from somewhere to stave off relegation to the competition’s second tier.

AI startup Serval is offering luxury vacations and referral bonuses to recruit engineers

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The company said it plans to hire more than 500 people over the next 12 months after raising $150 million at a $3 billion valuation

Serval is offering new engineering hires a paid luxury vacation worth up to $10,000, along with $30,000 referral bonuses, as the AI startup competes for technical talent.

The company detailed the vacation program in a blog post. New hires plan the trip one-on-one with a travel concierge through Serval's partner, Luxury Concierge Travel, and the first week on the job is paid, the company said. Serval's careers page says new hires can take the trip alone or bring a loved one.

The vacation applies to people who join the core engineering team or work as deployed software engineers, deployment strategists, deployment success engineers or automation engineers, according to the company.

Under the referral program, Serval pays $30,000 to an employee who refers an engineer who joins, and $30,000 to the new hire, the company said. The referral bonus comes on top of the vacation. Both perks are limited to engineering roles. The offer is open to U.S.-based applicants and is valid through Jan. 4, 2027.

Serval said it raised a $150 million Series C led by Sequoia at a $3 billion valuation. The company said it has grown its team tenfold and plans to hire more than 500 people over the next 12 months.

Serval launched a hiring push this fall aimed at bringing on 100 engineers, according to The Wall Street Journal. Carlos Lezama, Serval's head of talent, told The Wall Street Journal the company needs software engineers and deployment strategists. "Anthropic and OpenAI are taking a lot of talent," Lezama said.

The company said in its blog post that it wants new engineers to start work rested and that the vacation signals its hiring approach. It said the engineers it wants have many options.

Tencent's founder is willing to move half its workforce into AI-native businesses

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Tencent's public relations director said the 50% figure is an example of organizational change, not a layoff target

Tencent founder Pony Ma told employees he is willing to redeploy half of the company's personnel to AI-native businesses, according to Bloomberg.

Ma spoke at Tencent's 28th anniversary meeting on Wednesday, according to TokenPost. He described the company's main operations as an army, with its new AI work playing the part of a navy. "The army must leverage AI to maximize its efficiency, while we redeploy 50% of our personnel to the navy to support AI-native businesses," he said, according to Bloomberg.

Bloomberg said the speech seemed aimed at getting the entire workforce to adopt AI instead of announcing a major restructuring. Ma said one portion of Tencent, comprising half the company, would work on making current businesses run more efficiently, and the remaining portion would look for new ways to grow.

Tencent's public relations director said the 50% number was an example of organizational change rather than a goal for job cuts, according to TokenPost. Tencent has not publicly outlined any move to cut half of its staff, TokenPost said. Social media posts in China claimed that the company would cut half of its staff.

Ma also pushed for a less hierarchical setup with fewer layers of middle managers, according to Bloomberg. He said the push is meant to reshape the way teams operate and speed up decisions using the current workforce, setting it apart from previous cost-cutting and efficiency campaigns, according to TokenPost. He asked managers to limit drawn-out discussions with staff when handing out new assignments, TokenPost said.

It is not yet known how Tencent intends to restructure, according to Bloomberg. The company's last companywide reorganization came in 2018. Tencent had 115,927 employees as of June, up from 111,221 a year earlier.

Tencent's AI spending has climbed. Capital expenditures for AI projects and computing rose 176% to 52.8 billion yuan ($7.9 billion) in the quarter ended in June, as the company works to close ground on ByteDance and Alibaba.

Tencent stock has fallen about a third so far this year, which puts the company's valuation near $470 billion. Ma described Tencent as a "leaky ship" at a shareholder gathering in May, according to Bloomberg, after the company's Yuanbao chatbot failed to overtake ByteDance's Doubao and its work on large language models fell behind.

Tencent is scheduled to release its third-quarter results on Nov. 18. A Tencent spokesperson did not respond to Bloomberg's request for comment.

What Top Tri Coaches Fix Between November and March

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Top triathlon coaches share what they have their athletes work on in the off-season. (Photo: FinisherPix/Triathlete, Aaron Palaian, Oliver Baker, and courtesy David Tilbury-Davis)

One of triathlon’s attractions – and frustrations – is that there is always something to work on, and there are few better times to continue that never-ending quest for perfection than during the off-season.

With no race commitments, it gives triathletes the opportunity to step back, assess their strengths and weaknesses, and identify what to work on heading into spring.

For Northern Hemisphere athletes, that time is now. So, to help you along the way, we turned to four of the sport’s leading coaches to get their views — in their words — on what triathletes should focus on between now and March.

One of the world’s leading coaches and applied sports scientists in endurance sport, Plews has coached elite triathletes, including world champions Chelsea Sodaro, Javier Gomez, and Terenzo Bozzone.

He is also the founder of the coaching platform Endure IQ for both athletes and coaches. Plews himself is a former age-group Ironman world champion and still holds the fastest amateur time at the Hawaii Ironman World Championship.

“I never did proper strength training when I was younger, but now that I do a lot more, I’m seeing the benefits. But it is only as good as the quality and intent you put in. One of the big mistakes I see with many triathletes is that there’s no real intent.

“Get someone to help make sure you’re doing proper lifts. If you need to learn the correct movements, winter would be an ideal time. Then strength work needs to challenge the system. We typically talk “reps in reserve” when it comes to strength training [i.e., how many more reps you could complete before reaching failure].

“I’d advise working to two to three reps in reserve, but you can go to four if you’re not confident. But the main thing is you want to see a change in the speed of movement as you fatigue through the set.

“I would focus mostly on compound lifts: barbell hip thrusts, Bulgarian split squats, Nordic hamstring curls, and isometric holds and calf raises to add injury prevention. As time goes on, pair it with more plyometric and ballistic exercises, which might be a squat into a box jump.”

“Winter can be a period where you become unstuck from a psychological point of view. What’s important is to find balance. You want the volume, but don’t want to become stale so that by the time you get to the season, you’ve lost enthusiasm.

“If you really push the envelope early, especially with long, cold rides in January and February, by the time you get through to the season, you’re just sick of it. So, I think there’s a very fine line between doing too much training and not enough.”

The founder and head coach of NVDM Coaching in Austin, Texas, Van Der Merwe leads a team of more than 30 coaches supporting over 700 athletes. A former top-50 world-ranked junior tennis player from South Africa, she raced as a professional triathlete from 2010 to 2023 and has coached since 2009. She also leads Ironman U, Ironman’s official coaching certification program.

“Winter is when we raise the ceiling. We reverse periodize, so short, focused threshold and VO2 work comes before the long volume, because every triathlete races at a percentage of their FTP.

“If an athlete raises FTP from 220 to 235 watts this winter and rides an Ironman at 70% of it, race-day power goes from 154 to about 165 watts. Spring then becomes about extending that fitness.”

“Most athletes book a fit in spring, which leaves little time to adapt before racing. Our order is comfort first, then aerodynamics, then power, because the fastest position is the one you can hold for the whole bike leg and still run well afterward.

“In one study, the lowest torso angle tested cost riders about 16 watts at threshold compared with their own positions. Winter trainer rides are the perfect place to build into a new position gradually.”

“This is one most athletes never think about, and it can quietly derail a whole season. Vitamin D drops through the winter, especially for athletes training indoors at northern latitudes.

“A review of more than 2,000 athletes found over half had inadequate levels. Iron stores matter too, especially for female athletes. We ask athletes to get a baseline panel with vitamin D and ferritin now, so their doctor can address anything before the big training blocks start.”

Head coach at KIS Coaching, Cunnama is a former elite professional triathlete from South Africa who won major long-distance races, including Challenge Roth and Ironman Hamburg, and owns two top-five finishes at the Ironman World Championship in Kona. Following his racing career, he became a presenter with the Global Triathlon Network (GTN), sharing his knowledge and experience with a worldwide triathlon audience.

“Everyone knows they should work on their weaknesses over winter, but that usually translates to a broad goal like, ‘I want to work on my swim,’ which then turns into ‘more swimming, less biking and running.’

“I find it more effective to keep a balance in all three triathlon disciplines, but try to get ahead on the things that you always fell behind on last season. Determine which aspect of your training made you feel most underconfident, and then tweak your off-season training to target that area.

“For example, if you found that using paddles destroyed your arms, do extra paddle work. If hills were your nemesis on the bike, hit the hills (or simulate them on the trainer). Rather than tackling physical limitations, target the mental hurdles – use the off-season to get to a place where you love the sessions you hated last season.”

“It is hard to make changes to technique during the season when you have volume goals in training, because technique changes (or position changes on the bike) require you to only do as much of the new technique as you can sustain, and no more.

“You can’t ingrain a new technique when the second half of your session is falling back to the old technique! So, make those changes in the off-season and slowly build back that technique better. The advantage of triathlon is you have two other sports to keep you fit while you reset your technique in the third sport.”

A highly respected triathlon coach, Tilbury-Davis has worked with professional and age-group athletes since 1995. Through Tilbury-Davis Coaching, he has coached leading athletes such as Lionel Sanders and Ashleigh Gentle. Based in Finland, he works with a small group of international athletes.

“Efficiency of movement sounds vague, but while I’m a big proponent of doing strength and conditioning work throughout the year, winter is a time to understand that swimming, biking, and running all require expression of force from the core – when I say core, I effectively mean from the knees to the chest.

“You’re talking around 64 different muscles and creating the capacity to have a stable base to express force from will improve your body alignment in the water, your ability to maintain an aero position on the bike, and your ability to have hip extension and knee drive in the run. It centers around doing the kind of exercises that are multi-plane, multi-muscle recruiting as part of a several-times-a-week program.

“For example, you see a lot of triathletes train with a Masters group and the swim coach says to do a high elbow or chicken wing drill. The reality is that 99% of triathletes do not have sufficient mobility to swim in a manner that a swim coach would like. So, to step back, I would say to improve your swimming, go away and work on your thoracic and scapular mobility.”

“A lot of people don’t talk about saddle choice. We all buy expensive running shoes that are made out of expensive foam that breaks down over a period of time, but we forget that saddles are also made out of foam.

“If you’re serious about maintaining a good position on the bike, you probably should be changing your saddle once a year and for a pro that might be twice a year because the foam in the saddle breaks down the same way that the foam in a running shoe breaks down.”

“Get used to having to cope with uneven surfaces on the run, shortening and lengthening your stride length, stepping over rocks and roots, dealing with off camber, on camber, dealing with gravel, sand, mud, all of those proprioceptive stimuli that vary significantly improve your body’s ability to be efficient. A worst-case scenario for me would be an athlete that just runs on a treadmill the entire winter at a very narrow bandwidth of running paces.

“A good way of explaining this is that people talk about the way the Kenyans train. They do this famous long run in Iten where it’s a progressively descending pace. But what’s special about that workout is that they’re running on trails, gravel fire roads, wooded areas, and gradient changes. Probably the most powerful thing about that run is that they’re working on different surfaces, different terrains, different obstacles, different gradients, while they’re still working on a physiological stimulus.”

“That’s probably when people ask me what’s the number-one most important session for Ironman training: I would say go run on trails before anything else, because it builds resilience and strength, which is what you need for an Ironman.”

→ Baca artikel asli

‘Do not let Sauron have the ring’: Voorhees warns against a government veto on AI

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

His Sauron warning targets control over AI releases, a power already proposed in a September bill and El-Sayed’s campaign platform.

“Do not let Sauron have the ring,” Erik Voorhees, founder of AI service Venice, wrote on Nov. 12, warning against giving politicians control over artificial intelligence. His post as @ErikVoorhees targeted the concentration of political power over machine intelligence.

His warning followed a clip of All-In co-host David Sacks predicting a Democratic push for a federal department to approve frontier AI models before release. A September Sanders-Casar bill already proposes such a department and approval powers for covered advanced AI systems. El-Sayed's October Senate campaign platform proposes an agency that could block releases, with a different set of safeguards.

The clip published by @firesidealpha on Nov. 7 shows Sacks predicting a major Democratic push for AI legislation in the next Congress. He describes a federal AI department reviewing the most advanced models, giving developers its concerns and withholding release until those concerns are addressed.

Sacks goes further, alleging that Democrats want AI-powered surveillance and censorship. The proposals establish release-blocking powers, not the political motives he alleges.

The remarks came from the Nov. 7 All-In episode, announced by @theallinpod. In the full episode, Sacks also predicts a bargain in which government gains more power while closed-model companies gain regulatory protection. His concern therefore reaches beyond censorship to the possibility that compliance rules could protect established developers from competition.

Voorhees argues that open-source transparency and decentralization provide better safety, and that licensing undermines equality. His objection is to anyone holding that gatekeeping power.

Sen. Bernie Sanders and Rep. Greg Casar announced the Ban Artificial Superintelligence Act on Sept. 23, before Sacks's November remarks. The introduced Senate version, S.5493, would establish a Department of Artificial Intelligence, pause advanced AI development pending a regulatory framework and prohibit artificial superintelligence.

Its proposed approval mechanism is explicit. Section 9(d) would prevent a person from deploying, releasing, importing or transferring an advanced AI system before receiving the department's approval. The secretary would inspect for characteristics posing a danger to the public, including superintelligence and precursor characteristics, and withhold approval if dangerous characteristics are present.

The bill would also require a charter to develop or distribute advanced AI. Maintaining that charter would entail giving regulators access to systems, processes, staff and physical infrastructure. Separate provisions would require pauses and shutdown measures for specified dangerous systems.

This would put a federal decision between a covered developer and release of its model. It resembles the institutional mechanism Sacks describes much more closely than a voluntary testing arrangement does. It remains proposed legislation, however, rather than evidence that the next Congress has agreed to create such a system.

The bill also contains constraints on the regulator. It proposes Senate confirmation of the secretary, independent scientific advice and employee financial-interest restrictions. It provides Federal Circuit review of findings underlying charter revocations. That specific remedy is not a general appeal of every model-release decision.

Abdul El-Sayed's Oct. 13 “AI Under Control” platform calls for an independent, Cabinet-level agency empowered to inspect laboratories, block releases, order pauses and command shutdowns. The campaign document explicitly builds on the Sanders-Casar legislation.

It proposes freezing training and release of the most powerful systems until safety rules and enforcement exist, with independent scientists certifying safety before the pause lifts. It also calls for independent pre-release audits and mandatory breach reporting to government within 24 hours, accompanied by public summaries.

The safeguards are part of the proposal, too. A scientific board chosen outside the agency would manage its governance. Exemptions would be public, industry fees would fund the regulator and officials would be barred from later cashing in on the companies they regulate. The platform also supports whistleblower protections and preserving stronger state laws.

Crucially for Sacks's surveillance allegation, the document reiterates opposition to warrantless surveillance. Its stated prohibitions also include autonomous weapons firing and replacing human oversight in life-or-death decisions. The proposed release powers do not establish Sacks's allegation about the purpose they would serve.

Those protections do not eliminate the concentration of authority Voorhees opposes. An agency able to stop a release would retain that power even with outside scientific oversight. The safeguards seek to constrain its use, while leaving the regulator with a veto over covered releases.

The platform does not set out a detailed release-approval or appeal procedure. Its outside-selected scientific board also differs from S.5493's advisory board, whose members the secretary would select. The two proposals overlap in coercive powers while making different choices about who advises or governs the regulator.

Thanks for signing up to The Catalyst. Look out for your first daily email. If you already receive it, you’re all set.

Two proposals would give federal officials power over AI releases. Select one to see who would oversee the regulator.

Introduced legislation: Senate confirmation, scientific advice, financial restrictions and review of charter-revocation findings.

Campaign proposal: an outside-selected scientific board, public exemptions and limits on officials profiting from regulated companies.

Release-blocking powers are already proposed. The bill and campaign platform put different safeguards around that authority.

Both are proposals, not an enacted common system. S.5493 covers defined advanced AI. The Federal Circuit route shown concerns findings behind charter revocations, not general release-approval appeals. The campaign platform does not detail an approval or appeal procedure.

Qualitative schematic of selected powers and safeguards from introduced S.5493 and El-Sayed's October 13 platform. Lines represent authority, advice or review; lengths and areas carry no quantitative meaning.

Both are proposals, not an enacted common system. S.5493 covers defined advanced AI. The Federal Circuit route shown concerns findings behind charter revocations, not general release-approval appeals. The campaign platform does not detail an approval or appeal procedure.

Sacks's reference to the next Congress comes against an election shift. CryptoSlate, citing Associated Press, reported that Democrats had won the House and unified control of Congress in its election coverage, updated Nov. 8.

Existing federal policy also needs to be distinguished from the proposed approval regime. Trump's June 2 executive order directs a voluntary framework for government access to frontier models. Section 3(c) expressly says that section does not authorize mandatory government licensing, preclearance or permitting for new models. That limitation concerns the framework in that order, rather than every other government power over AI.

For Voorhees, the issue is longstanding and tied to his business. His July 2024 introduction to Venice identifies him as founder and CEO, describes an application using leading open-source models and argues against corporate or government monopoly over intelligence. CryptoSlate also covered the connection between model-access restrictions and decentralized AI in June.

The next legislative test is whether these proposals gain committee action and leadership backing, and what any new text says about release criteria, decision-making authority and review of adverse decisions.

Alphabet and Meta Platforms Pay Dividends. Is Either of Them Worth Owning for Income?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Both of these internet heavyweights announced quarterly dividends in the first half of 2024.

Both Alphabet (GOOGL +0.68%) (GOOG +0.38%) and Meta Platforms (META +0.01%) instituted a notable change to their capital allocation policies in 2024. The social media giant moved first. In February of that year, it introduced a $0.50 quarterly dividend. The Google parent followed suit, announcing a quarterly payout of $0.20 in April 2024.

These companies have been wonderful to their shareholders over the years. Both have outperformed the S&P 500 index in the past decade. Capital appreciation was the main driver of returns in investment portfolios.

But now that dividends are a mainstay, do either of these "Magnificent Seven" stocks make sense for investors seeking income-generating opportunities?

These companies have raised their dividends in the past couple of years. Alphabet currently pays $0.22 per share, up 10% from the initial distribution. Meta Platforms' dividend is up 5% from the beginning to $0.525 today. While the growth is certainly small, these leadership teams are at least hinting to shareholders that slowly increasing the payouts might be a permanent feature.

There is minimal risk that these dividends come under any pressure. Both businesses have historically operated from a position of financial robustness. In the past five years, Alphabet's quarterly operating margin averaged a fantastic 30.4%. At 36.4%, Meta Platforms' average margin is even better. And combined, they have $67.5 billion in cash and cash equivalents on their balance sheets.

Dividends are well covered by their earnings streams. Through the first nine months of 2026, Alphabet's net income was almost 25 times higher than its dividends, while Meta Platforms' bottom line was nearly 13 times greater than what it paid in distributions to shareholders. From this vantage point, it looks like the payouts are safe.

However, investors shouldn't rush to buy these businesses if all they're after is a meaningful income stream. The dividend yields are tiny. Alphabet, at a dividend yield of 0.31%, and Meta Platforms, at 0.33%, aren't attracting any attention in this regard. These figures are significantly lower than the inflation rate.

There are superior choices out there. The Vanguard S&P 500 ETF, for example, offers a much better dividend yield of 1.07%. More stable consumer stocks, like Coca-Cola, Procter & Gamble, and Altria Group, pay investors sizable amounts. And it's hard to ignore the 10-year Treasury, which currently yields 5%.

Given how small their dividend yields are, Alphabet and Meta Platforms don't exactly cater to income seekers. On the other hand, they still target investors who want to add growth opportunities to their portfolios. These businesses offer the potential for market-beating returns over the next five or 10 years.

Their scale is not an inhibitor. Alphabet posted 25% year-over-year revenue growth in Q3, similar to Meta Platforms' 26% gain. This points to long expansionary runways ahead. These companies are direct beneficiaries of the internet's proliferation.

These management teams have been directing their time and resources to artificial intelligence (AI), which is sucking up capital that might otherwise have gone to raising dividend payouts. Both Alphabet and Meta Platforms will invest hundreds of billions of dollars this year in technical infrastructure to expand compute capacity and support their AI initiatives.

And they're raising external funding to power these ambitions, in turn pausing share buybacks, which historically played a key role in returning capital to investors. Free cash flow has also turned negative. This development will test the market's patience because nobody knows the kinds of returns these substantial AI-related investments will bring. It's impossible to predict when the data center spending boom will peak or who will capture the excess profits.

On an earnings basis, however, these two leading technology stocks trade at very compelling valuations. Market sentiment reflects elevated levels of uncertainty.

When our analyst team has a stock tip, it can pay to listen. After all, Stock Advisor's total average return is 835%* — a market-crushing outperformance compared to 208% for the S&P 500.

They just revealed what they believe are the 10 best stocks for investors to buy right now, available when you join Stock Advisor.

Jensen Huang Resigns as Nvidia CEO, With CFO Colette Kress Named Interim Chief

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Jensen Huang Resigns as Nvidia CEO, With CFO Colette Kress Named Interim Chief

Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO of Nvidia, ending a 33-year tenure at the helm of the chipmaker he co-founded, after reports of affairs with a suspected Chinese spy and a senior company executive.

Huang has stepped down as president, CEO and a member of the board, effective immediately, Nvidia said on Thursday. Colette Kress, Nvidia's chief financial officer since 2013, will serve as interim CEO while remaining CFO, as the board searches for a permanent successor from both inside and outside the company. Nvidia did not give a reason for Huang's resignation, but thanked him for his "immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry."

NVIDIA announces CEO transition. Read the release: https://t.co/MTTUvFHCJA

— NVIDIA Newsroom (@nvidianewsroom) November 12, 2026

The board also named Harvey Jones, a former CEO and executive chairman of chip design software company Synopsys, as its first-ever chair.

"The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company's operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption," Jones said.

Kress said she was "committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years." Nvidia will report its third-quarter results as scheduled on Tuesday, the company said.

Nvidia shares were down 7.7% at $160.28 shortly after the market opened on Thursday, having fallen as low as $148.80, according to Yahoo! Finance data.

Huang's departure comes four days after he acknowledged in a letter to staff that he had been unfaithful to his wife, prompting Nvidia's board to open an independent investigation. He had told the board that he had been intimate with Alice Huang, an executive at Singapore-based Nvidia customer Megaspeed, The New York Times reported.

The Times had reported in October that U.S. officials had concluded Alice Huang was a Chinese intelligence asset, while the Financial Times later reported that a U.S. government memo said the two had been romantically involved. China has denied the allegations.

On Wednesday, the Times reported that Huang had also had a relationship with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of health care, who reports to him. The Wall Street Journal later reported that the board was preparing to oust him, adding to the pressure on the CEO.

The scandal has wiped hundreds of billions of dollars off Nvidia's market value, with the company losing about $430 billion in two days earlier this week. Nvidia and Huang also face a proposed shareholder class action, and lawmakers had threatened to subpoena him.

Huang ran Nvidia with about 60 executives reporting directly to him and has said he did not believe in succession planning, leaving the company without an obvious successor.

Prior to the announcement of Huang's resignation, one person inside the company had told the Financial Times that naming Kress as interim CEO would be the simplest option. Other potential candidates cited by the paper's sources include Ian Buck, who runs Nvidia's data center business, and Arm CEO Rene Haas, a former Nvidia executive.

Huang remains Nvidia's largest shareholder, with a stake of about 3.6%, according to the Journal.

When you find and earn one of the many MW4 DMZ keys, here's where you need to go to use it

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Whether you've earned one of the various MW4 DMZ keys by completing a Story Mission, finding it somewhere on the map, or, dare I say, by killing another player (shame on you), then you might be wondering where to use it.In MW4's DMZ mode, there are loads of keys that unlock valuable lockers and vaults, but their description only gets you to a general area - not the precise location of the wares. To help you out, here's a full list of every DMZ key and where to use it, so if you're looking something in particular, find it with CTRL+F or your browser's search function.

These are all the keys that can be used in the Political Prison area of the map:

Some of the keys can be used at different locations, including the likes of Blackmarket Weapons Lockers and Silo Control Rooms, both of which you can find in this northwest part of the map. This is a common spawn area, as well, so if you have one of these multi-use keys, this is a good place to use it.

Keys 5 to 11 in this list are a little harder to use as you'll need to breach the actual prison, and you'll of course encounter plenty of hostility as you do so.

These are all the DMZ keys that can be used in the Cheongun Village and Mirae Hospital areas of the map:

The village is a fairly simple area to loot as it's lots of small buildings rather than one well-locked down area like the hospital or the casino. The casino is a particularly tough location to get into, so make sure you're well kitted out with your best MW4 guns and a medkit or two.

Speaking of the Casino, the Counting Room Vault key is one of the better keys you'll get early on if you're completing Story Missions, and we've got a handy guide to getting into the DMZ Counting Room Vault if you'd like some help.

Sign up to our Switch 2 Spotlight newsletter to make sure you get the very latest news on Nintendo's new console and its games every week

These are the keys that can be used in the Hanuel Nuclear Reactor area of the map:

The Nuclear Reactor area is the hardest location to enter as you'll be faced with a radiation field that will quickly deal you damage if you're not wearing a gas mask. You're still not immune even with a gas mask, though, as they deteriorate in time, so plan your entry into this area carefully, and keep any keys safely in your stash until you're fully ready for the challenge.

These are the keys that can be used in the Songdo Wharf and The Grid areas of the map:

This area is a good place to use any DMZ bank vault keys, as explained in more detail in our standalone guide, as there are three different banks here you can attempt to access (including the one slightly further south, as you'll see below). One is the main bank tower located inside The Grid, though, which itself is a high-threat area, so enter at your own risk.

The following keys are used in the Wolves Stadium and Hajin River Heights areas of the map:

As I've mentioned above, you've got a bank vault on the boundary between this area and The Grid above it, plus another one to the south of the stadium, both of which are pretty easy to access with the right materials. Meanwhile, another of the DMZ police stations is located here, so if you've got both keys and a big enough backpack, it's a great place to hit.

These are the keys that you can use in the Imjin Farmland area of the map:

There isn't much need to come to this area unless you have any of the specific keys above as it's quite an isolated area with not much else going on. Other than the Fire Station Storage key, though, these are all unique to this area, so if you find any, you might want to collect them and hit the area in one go. You may even find some others while you're looking around, so dedicate your entire match to this zone and have a good search.

These are all the keys that are used in the Air Base and Deadtown areas of the map:

This western area of the map is another common spawn area, so if you've got a Blackmarket Locker key or Silo Control Room key, this is a good place to consider. There are also plenty of good weapons to grab around here, so take your time and explore carefully.

American Airlines Pilot Arrested At Heathrow For Being Unfit To Fly And Having Cocaine

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Every so often we’ll hear stories of pilots being arrested for showing up at the airport intoxicated. Well, here’s an even wilder story, about an American Airlines pilot who not only showed up at the airport unfit to fly, but also had cocaine on him.

View from the Wing flags a story about how on November 1, 2026, a 64-year-old pilot was arrested at London Heathrow Airport (LHR):

So this is sort of wild — not only was he under the influence of something (drugs or alcohol), but then he also had cocaine in his possession. That’s just next level irresponsibility.

It’s not clear how exactly all of this was discovered, in terms of the sequence of events. Was there just a random check, or was he acting erratically, authorities were alerted, and that’s when this all came to light? If so, it sounds like the first clue was how he was acting (which made him unfit to fly), and then presumably he was searched, which is when the cocaine may have been discovered.

This is just so beyond irresponsible. If he was already unfit to fly during boarding and had cocaine in his possession, was he going to consume that during the flight, or just take it home with him?

Keep in mind that in the United States, commercial pilots have to retire by their 65th birthday. So this guy was just weeks or months from retirement… what a sad way to end a career, as you can bet he won’t be flying again. One wonders if he was just trying to party a little before retirement, if this had been going on for some time, or what.

While the authorities haven’t identified the airline that this pilot works for (other than to say he works for a US airline), it’s not too hard to figure out. Based on his name (which I won’t post here, because he’s in enough trouble as is), it’s clear he’s an American Airlines pilot.

American had two flight cancelations out of Heathrow that day — one for flight AA85 to Chicago (ORD), and one for flight AA91 to Miami (MIA). It’s not clear which of those he was scheduled to fly on.

Under UK261 regulations, any passenger who ended up arriving at their final destination four or more hours late would be entitled to £520 ($690) in cash compensation. That’s on top of covering any reasonable expenses incurred. Admittedly some people may have been rebooked on other flights once this flight was axed, but at least a large percentage of passengers would presumably be eligible for this.

An American Airlines pilot who was close to retirement decided to end his career in a rather unfortunate way, as he was arrested at Heathrow for being unfit to fly, while also having cocaine in his possession. The lack of decent judgment shown here is beyond irresponsible, and I hope he gets the help he needs so he doesn’t ever endanger others in this way again. The good news for him is that he was within months of retirement anyway, so at least his “career” impact is pretty minimal.

What do you make of this American pilot being arrested?

Jensen Huang Resigns: NVDA Crashes, Sending Shockwaves to The Crypto Market

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Ahmed Barakat is a journalist and copywriter based in Georgia with a growing focus on blockchain technology, DeFi, AI, privacy, digital assets, and fintech innovation.

The CryptoNews editorial team is composed of seasoned writers specializing in cryptocurrency and blockchain technology. Their expertise ensures comprehensive, accurate, and insightful content for...

Nvidia (NVDA) shares plunged nearly 10% as CEO Jensen Huang resigns, triggering a wave of selling across technology and crypto markets. Bitcoin also tumbled below $72,000 as investors pulled back from risk assets.

Huang’s departure ends a 33-year leadership run at the company he co-founded in 1993. Nvidia’s board confirmed his immediate resignation as president, CEO, and board director minutes ago.

BREAKING: Nvidia, $NVDA, CEO Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO, President, and member of the Board of Directors, effective immediately. pic.twitter.com/IJE8wWvcrR

The company appointed Chief Financial Officer Colette Kress as interim CEO while beginning its search for a permanent replacement. Harvey C. Jones, former Synopsys CEO, will become Nvidia’s first-ever board chairman.

The leadership shake-up has rattled investors across multiple markets. Nvidia sits at the center of the artificial intelligence boom, making its sudden leadership change a major shock for technology stocks and crypto traders.

Just minutes after Jensen Huang resigns, Nvidia’s sell-off quickly spread into crypto derivatives markets, where traders can access tokenized exposure to traditional stocks around the clock. NVDA perpetual futures on Hyperliquid plunged nearly 12% to $160.99 as news of Huang’s departure spread.

Similar losses appeared across centralized exchanges, including Binance, OKX, and Bybit. Cumulative trading volume across Nvidia perpetual futures markets has surpassed $7.8 billion, according to CoinGlass. Binance accounted for approximately 44% of that activity.

Nvidia-linked contracts have also become a significant market on Hyperliquid. They ranked fourth in open interest, behind Bitcoin, Ethereum, and HYPE futures. This growing exposure puts decentralized derivatives traders directly in the path of major developments involving Wall Street’s biggest companies.

Uncertainty grows around Nvidia’s AI ambitions. The company supplies critical computing hardware for major technology firms, cloud providers, and artificial intelligence developers. The disruption has, as expected, influenced sentiment across the entire AI investment trade.

Discover: The Crypto Presales This Q4 2026

Bitcoin extended its decline on Thursday, dropping below $72,000 as selling pressure spread across cryptocurrency markets. Ethereum also weakened, trading under $1,900 as investors reduced exposure to riskier assets. https://cryptonews.com/news/ethereum-price-2000-scoop-hold-dump/

Bitcoin’s decline also comes as Nvidia-linked derivatives expose crypto traders to volatility originating outside traditional digital assets. The relationship between AI stocks and crypto markets has become increasingly important as institutional investors move between technology equities and digital assets.

With Bitcoin now below $72,000 and NVDA facing a major leadership transition, both markets are entering a critical trading period.

Lionel Richie Breaks Silence Nearly 2 Months After Heart Procedure

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Lionel Richie was happy to report he’s back during his Nov. 11 residency concert at The Wynn in Las Vegas, occurring two months after he underwent a heart procedure.

It’s Lionel Richie fans have been looking for, and he’s back to say hello.

The 77-year-old took a moment of his Nov. 11 residency performance at The Wynn in Las Vegas to address his recent health scare—during which time he underwent a heart procedure—assuring fans he was ready to boogie all night long.

“I’m back,” an increasingly emphatic Richie told the crowd three times during the show, before quipping, “I don’t know where I was, but I’m back.”

“I’m doing just fine,” he emphasized, as seen in a video shared by @jpasc24 on Instagram. “Thank you all so much for caring about me.”

And with the arms of the crowd open wide in welcome, Richie decided to turn the evening into one of celebration—with one specific honoree in mind.

“I want to make it a tribute tonight, to me,” he exclaimed. “I better make a tribute to myself before somebody else has that show down the street, ‘Come see Lionel Richie down the street!’”

But why stop at just one night? As Richie noted, “Wait a minute, no, this is gonna be a whole week of celebrating Lionel Richie.”

However, after some dancing on the ceiling—or, in this case, some jumping around the stage—a winded Richie took a moment to refuel.

“That was too much, that was beyond my electrolyte capabilities,” he said, after which a crew member brought Richie a glass of red electrolytes. “Now let me tell y’all something, for years I thought that water was important. And then, one day, I got dizzy and I said bring me some water, and the man said, ‘You don’t need water, you need electrolytes.’”

“All these years, drinking water,” Richie joked. “So now I drink electrolytes for breakfast, lunch and dinner, and then the doctor told me you need to eat some food. And I said, ‘Well, the last food I ate, the chickens, was contaminated, then the lettuce became contaminated, so what the hell are you supposed to eat?’”

But thankfully, the electrolytes seemed to do the trick.

“Now that I feel refreshed,” a reenergized Richie said, walking towards his piano, “I’m gonna play a song.”

Richie’s onstage celebration comes just under two months since he underwent a routine heart procedure to treat Atrial fibrillation, commonly known as Afib.

At the time, his rep shared in a Sept. 22 statement, “The doctor said everything went perfectly. Lionel is doing great but will take some time off and will be back on stage soon.”

But while Richie is fully on the mend, he’s not the only celebrity to have had to navigate a challenging health scare. Read on for more.

The Jessie alum opened up about her "hellish" migraines, sharing how they've worsened after suffering five concussions over the course of her life.

"The brain injuries make things significantly worse," Ryan shared during a September 2026 episode of Mythical Kitchen’s Last Meals. "I had a pretty crazy, life-altering brain injury a few years ago, and I was out of commission for six months."

While Ryan described her first four concussions as "incidents," she said her fifth one was trauma caused by "sustained shaking over a minute of time."

Now, the former Disney Channel star needs to close her eyes to "think better."

"Quite often the world is really loud. It’s really too bright. There’s a lot going on," she explained. "The visual data that I'm taking in can be so distracting and so loud. Then, with the migraines—which are significantly worsened by that—it sort of is too much."

Brianna "Chickenfry" LaPaglia's Ovarian Cyst

While the internet isn't always a kind place, Amy Schumer is happy that, in this case, it helped her get answers."The internet really came for me after doing a bunch of press, and I was like, 'OK everybody, relax,'" Amy recalled of her "puffier" face on a January 2025 episode of Call Her Daddy. And I’m like, ‘Wait, I’m getting trolled by doctors?’"After these doctors said they thought she had Cushing syndrome and that it may be caused by spiking coritsol levels or steroid injections, the Life & Beth creator thought, "Wait, I have been getting steroid injections in my scars.""I had a breast reduction, a C-section, whatever, and so I was getting these steroid injections," Amy said. And then he asked me again and when I went to respond, I couldn't speak. The right side of my face started drooping. I couldn't get a sentence out."While Hailey said the facial drooping stopped and her speech came back, she went to the hospital to make sure she was OK."They did some scans and they were able to see that I had suffered a small blood clot to my brain," the model added, "which they labeled and categorized as something called a TIA [Transient Ischemic Attack]."Later, Hailey went to the University of California, Los Angeles, where she found out she had a hole in her heart called a patent foramen ovale (PFO).She explained the blood clot had traveled into her heart, "escaped" through the hole and went to her brain, leading to the TIA.Hailey said she had a successful PFO closure procedure and was now feeling great.

But after a while, the paralysis went away."He's doing really well," Hailey said on a June 2022 episode of Good Morning America, later adding, "He's feeling a lot better. Obviously, it was just a very scary and random situation to happen, but he's going to be totally OK and I'm just grateful that he's fine."

Tracey Yukich's Medical Emergency on The Biggest Loser

The reality star revealed that she "died" while running a mile during the first challenge of the NBC competition series.

“I don't remember a lot,” she explained on Fit for TV: The Reality of The Biggest Loser. “I remember hearing the helicopter. I just felt like I was floating. And then my grandpa was there. And then I saw darkness. But then I saw light. So I knew, I knew I died that day.”

“My organs were literally shutting down,” she continued. “I didn't realize that I had rhabdomyolysis. And rhabdomyolysis is your body's way of saying, ‘I'm going to shut down on you.’”

Emilia Clarke filmed battle scenes for Game of Thrones, but in 2019, she published an essay in The New Yorker titled "A Battle for My Life."Having a bad headache at the gym, "I reached the toilet, sank to my knees, and proceeded to be violently, voluminously ill," the actress wrote. "Meanwhile, the pain—shooting, stabbing, constricting pain—was getting worse. At some level, I knew what was happening: my brain was damaged."She was taken to the hospital for a brain scan."The diagnosis was quick and ominous: a subarachnoid hemorrhage (SAH), a life-threatening type of stroke, caused by bleeding into the space surrounding the brain," the Emmy nominee added. "I’d had an aneurysm, an arterial rupture."Emilia had immediate surgery to seal the aneurysm, calling the pain "unbearable." While she was recovering, she continued, she experienced aphasia and was "muttering nonsense."A week later, "the aphasia passed," Emilia added, and she left the hospital a month after being admitted. At a 2013 brain scan, she learned a growth "doubled in size" and that she needed surgery again."When they woke me, I was screaming in pain," she wrote. "The procedure had failed. I had a massive bleed and the doctors made it plain that my chances of surviving were precarious if they didn’t operate again. This time they needed to access my brain in the old-fashioned way—through my skull."Thankfully, Emilia shared, she's now "at a hundred per cent."

News correspondent Will Marfuggi in December 2024. "Everybody just came to my rescue."Because of other medical concerns, Cori ended up staying in the hospital longer than she initially expected."I could have went home after my stroke, but my lupus wasn't doing so well," the Snoop's Fatherhood: Cori and Wayne's Story star continued. "My kidneys were failing. It was a lot going on."Today, Cori is on the mend."I'm doing great," she added. "I just had a little bit of motor skills that I had to work back on. But other than that, I was fine."

Shailene Woodley’s early 20s were not an easy time health-wise.“It got to the point where I was losing my hearing. I couldn’t walk for longer than five minutes at a time without having to lay down for hours, and hours and sleep. Everything I ate hurt my stomach,” the actress said on a September 2024 episode of the SHE MD Podcast. “It was this conflation of issues and diagnoses and different doctors telling me different things.”So Shailene—who chose to keep the exact condition private—set out to find answers. “I come from a very holistic background and study herbalism,” she added. “So I was very keen on, ‘I’m going to work with real MDs, [and] I’m also going to work with independent kind of healers.’ Just trying to search for some sense of comfort in my own skin.”It was a long journey—one that lasted for 10 years.“Throughout that decade, a lot of other things came from feeling so much discomfort physically,” the Big Little Lies star noted, “which was, ‘My gosh, if everything I eat hurts my stomach, I’m now suddenly afraid of food.’ And then going into the kind of mental f--ckery that can happen with that of body dysmorphia and confusion about identity and feeling safe in my own capsule, in my own skin, what that meant, and what that should be.”After tending to both her physical and mental health, Shailene is feeling much better.“It was a journey that ultimately physically resolved itself, and I am very healthy,” she shared. “I’m so happy to be able to say that."

“It’s a special place” – Liverpool confirm Anfield return for James Milner

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Liverpool FC have confirmed on Thursday that James Milner will return to Anfield in 2027.

Signed on a free transfer from Manchester City in 2015, the former Reds midfielder spent eight years on Merseyside and became vice-captain under Jurgen Klopp, winning the Premier League and Champions League among other trophies in his time with the club.

The 40-year-old closed out his playing career at Brighton, announcing his retirement this summer after setting the record for the most Premier League seasons appeared in (24 consecutively from 2002/03 to 2025/26).

As announced via liverpoolfc.com on Thursday lunchtime, Milner is the first player to be confirmed for the next Liverpool Legends match, which takes place against Inter Milan at Anfield on Saturday 27 March 2027, with proceeds going towards the LFC Foundation.

The Reds’ former vice-captain said of his participation in the fixture: “I’m thrilled to have the chance to pull on the red shirt again and step back out at Anfield. It’s a special place, and playing there in front of the supporters is a feeling like no other.

“What makes the legends match so special is that it’s about more than football. It’s a chance to give something back to the fans while supporting the work of the LFC Foundation. I’m delighted to be the first to sign up and can’t wait to be part of it.”

Want more Empire of the Kop coverage? Add us as a preferred source on Google to your favourites list for news you can trust

The news of Milner’s participation in the 2027 Legends match is sure to go down a treat with Liverpool fans who adored him for his humility and commitment to the cause in his eight years as a Red, along with his excellence as a player.

The Yorkshire native was renowned for setting exceptionally high standards within the playing group and enjoyed near-annual domination of the infamous pre-season lactate test, and he commands universal respect among his peers and the wider football community.

The Reds were lucky enough to be the club that he represented more times than any other, with 322 of his 903 senior appearances coming for the Merseysiders (Transfermarkt).

He’ll now get to line out in a Liverpool shirt at Anfield once more, and while it mightn’t be in a competitive fixture, the reception won’t be any less rapturous for a player whose supreme attitude characterised a glorious era for LFC.

As ever, it promises to be a wonderful occasion in which thousands of pounds will be raised for a most worthy cause, and Kopites will get to enjoy seeing many former greats of the club return for one afternoon.

S&P 500 falls 0.8% and Nasdaq 1.5% as Nvidia sinks after Huang’s resignation

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

Nvidia stock fell more than 11% after CFO Colette Kress was named interim CEO, while oil rose on President Donald Trump's Iran remarks

Nvidia $NVDA said on Thursday that CEO Jensen Huang has resigned and named CFO Colette Kress interim CEO, and the company's stock fell more than 11%.

The S&P 500 fell 0.8% and the Nasdaq $NDAQ Composite lost 1.5%. The Dow Jones Industrial Average gained 0.2%, a rise of 108 points.

Nvidia stock slid by up to 14% when trading began. Kress will serve until the company finds a permanent successor. Huang co-founded Nvidia in 1993 and has previously dismissed the value of mapping out a successor, according to The Wall Street Journal.

Semiconductor shares and other artificial-intelligence plays declined in Asia. According to The Wall Street Journal, Nvidia’s board had been planning to oust Huang. The Journal also said the slide put Nvidia on track to have lost just shy of $750 billion in market value since the scandal broke.

Huang had acknowledged an affair with a woman suspected of spying for China, according to CNBC.

Investors shifted money toward other chipmakers. Shares of Advanced Micro Devices and Broadcom $AVGO each rose around 2%. Oracle $ORCL stock lost 4%.

Oil prices climbed following a Truth Social post in which President Donald Trump declared, "We don't need a deal with Iran. Those days have long since passed." U.S. West Texas Intermediate futures were up by over 1%, changing hands at more than $87 a barrel. Brent crude futures also were up over 1%, topping $94.

Chevron $CVX and ExxonMobil $XOM stock each rose roughly 1%. Eli Lilly $LLY stock gained more than 1%.

Mariska Hargitay on Filming 600 Episodes of ‘Law & Order: SVU,’ Benson’s New Journey and Revisiting Her ‘Intense History’ With Stabler

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Mariska Hargitay is capping off her stellar year with yet another milestone: 600 episodes of “Law & Order: Special Victims Unit,” the NBC crime drama on which she’s starred as the indomitable Captain Olivia Benson since 1999. The hour, titled “Obedience,” airs on Nov. 12 and is highlighted by the return of Benson’s former partner Elliot Stabler, played by Christopher Meloni. Since his departure in 2011, Meloni has popped in for a handful of guest appearances, but this time was special. Related Stories ‘How to Rob a Bank’ Review: Nicholas Hoult and Zoë Kravitz Take Their Paychecks in an Annoyingly Flashy, Emotionally Turgid Influencer-Age Heist Movie

For Hargitay, the months leading up to the shoot were wildly eventful: In September, she took home two Emmys for her documentary “My Mom Jayne”; the following week, she hosted the show; then she flew right back to New York and started filming Jalen Brunson’s episode of “SVU” the following day. Four days after that ended, production on Episode 600 began.

So when it came time for the celebration dinner, Hargitay nearly passed. “I wanted to go home and go to bed,” she says. But like the leader she is, she went anyway. Huddled around two tables was the current cast and crew, creator Dick Wolf, Wolf Entertainment chief operating officer Peter Jankowski and, of course, Meloni.

“It was one of the most extraordinary, meaningful nights, and unexpectedly so to me,” Hargitay recalls. At the start of the evening, “Dick got up and said, ‘This has never happened, and it will never happen again.’ And everything just landed that night.”

Hargitay was asked to give a toast, which she read in full during our conversation, shedding tears as she spoke.

“Here we are at episode 600, and it really doesn’t feel totally real — which in a way is exactly how it should feel, because it’s as if we all get to live in this dream of what you thought might be possible someday,” her speech began. “And you’ve shown that it’s not only possible, but it’s so much better than anyone could have imagined.”

The episode was also better than she could have imagined, with the hour tackling the always fraught relationship between Benson and Stabler as they reopen an old case. Plus there are a few scenes, she says, that include “fun moments when they kind of mirror Mariska and Chris.”

Jankowski says Meloni “will always be a part of the show’s DNA,” no matter how long he’s been away. “There is something reassuring about seeing Mariska and Chris work together just like old times. The difference is that for the last 15 years it has been Mariska’s show. She is the alpha dog now.”

Hargitay took over at the top of the call sheet after Meloni’s exit and stayed there. As both star and executive producer, she now takes home nearly $850,000 an episode. She has also, through her Joyful Heart Foundation, helped to shift the narrative around sexual assault and given victims a voice they felt they’d lost.

The 600th episode leans into Benson’s personal journey in a way the show never has. “It’s about a woman having her voice and being submissive or subservient in a way, or giving up our power or our instinct, and that’s an important story to tell,” she says. “Because so many women identify with that.”

The episode also unpacks her dynamic with Stabler and their “intense history,” which makes working together very complicated. “Every relationship is fraught. Every relationship has power dynamics, and will they/won’t they, and what could have been and what isn’t and why, and could they and can’t they, and the complexity of all that.”

And it all works onscreen: “Some things are a mystery,” she adds, “and I think chemistry is one.”

Hargitay ended her toast to the “SVU” family by telling Wolf, “You truly have television — the kind of vision that lets you see and dream and believe far enough and wide enough to create some of this. All those years ago, when you sat there with this idea for the show, and you said, ‘Huh, this might work,’ you used your television, your foresight, to look many years in the future.”

Jankowski adds that while so much in the TV landscape has shifted and is changing every day, sometimes the right people can overcome the impossible. “One hundred episodes used to be the measuring stick of success on network TV,” he says, “which makes ‘SVU’’s 600 episodes almost surreal.”

NBC Entertainment chairman Pearlena Igbokwe says what’s even more “remarkable” than the episode number is the impact the show continues to have.

“Dick Wolf created a series with a rare ability to evolve with the world around it while never losing sight of the humanity at the heart of its stories. Mariska Hargitay as Olivia Benson is more than an iconic television character,” she says. “She represents the kind of strength, empathy and courage we all hope to have on our side. So many extraordinary artists have contributed to ‘SVU’ over the years, and together they’ve created a legacy that is truly unmatched.”

“Law & Order: SVU” airs on NBC Thursdays at 9 p.m. ET.

DPC Holdings (DPC) Q3 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Five long-term partnerships and a Montana Precision stake shape the growth story.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

DPC Holdings PLC (DPC +8.81%) reported record financial results for the third quarter of 2026, led by a 39% increase in revenue and a 56% rise in adjusted EBITDA. Management attributed the performance to strong demand in the aerospace and industrial gas turbine (IGT) end markets, which both grew over 40% during the period. The company emphasized its strategic transition toward long-term customer partnerships, announcing a fifth major agreement that includes significant customer funding for capacity expansion. These partnerships, combined with the acquisition of a 50% stake in Montana Precision Products, are intended to provide long-term revenue visibility and enhance returns on invested capital. The divestment of the noncore Ivostud business further focuses the portfolio on high-growth engine products and superalloys.

Moving to our IGT offering.

We make turbine airfoils mainly for heavy duty gas turbines. I. E. Those that are producing over 100 megawatts of power. The turbines have extremely high blade complexity and are used for utility scale generation. They are differentiated from small to medium industrial gas turbines, where we are seeing new entrants moving into the market. Such as those players looking to internalize their own supply. The smaller turbines and aero derivatives variations have a faster installation setup So many are being installed in tandem to power data centers until larger, more efficient heavy duty frame turbines can be supplied.

The benefits of heavy frame versus smaller turbines are better economies of scale, higher fuel efficiencies, increasingly important to generate significant dollar savings, provide stronger grid support, and have lower maintenance complexity. So I have said Doncaster specializes in heavy duty frame gas turbines accounting for approximately 85% of our IGT casting revenue. This is where we have significant demand, and so we are planning to double our heavy frame capacity given our contracted LTAs and strategic customer partnerships. I would like to clarify that. Especially on the IGT side. Our strategic customer partnerships, all have customer funding and volume commitments, including a take or pay clause. Meaning a capacity growth and earnings opportunity is supported by large OEMs.

Right? You know, not to get too technical on the call, but there are 4 stages in the turbine and those blades and veins, particularly in stages 1 and 2, which run at 1.5 thousand°C, they need to be replaced, you know, multiple times, 3 to 4 times during the life cycle of the engine.

When you go out to the cooler section Stage 3 maybe 2 to 3 times, sorry, and 2 to 3 times, sorry, and stage 4, twice. that is a very long term recurring revenue stream once these units get installed. And, of course, that is great for Doncasters because we do not differentiate between an OEM and an aftermarket or a replacement spare part that goes into these turbines. So just because we ship 1 set when we ship the turbine, we know we are getting these repeat orders for many, many years to come. You know, and that. Because the fleet is so new, we are just getting in just at the very start of those replacement cycles.

Jensen Huang officially resigns as Nvidia CEO

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

For the first time in its 33-year history, Nvidia has a new CEO. Jensen Huang, who co-founded the chipmaker in 1993 and led it through the AI boom, resigned Thursday. He also stepped down as president and gave up his seat on the board, effective immediately.

Nvidia gave no reason for his departure, though we can think of a few (namely, two).

For now, the job will go to CFO of Nvidia, Colette Kress, who has run Nvidia's finances since 2013. She'll stay on as chief financial officer while serving as interim CEO, and the board will look both inside and outside the company for a permanent replacement. The board also picked longtime director Harvey C. Jones to serve as its first chair.

The announcement came a day after Nvidia's board met to discuss Huang's future. Why? Earlier Nov. 11, the Times reported that Huang had an affair with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of healthcare and one of his direct reports. Though that wasn't the only affair. The best tech deal this week The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile Products available for purchase through affiliate links. If you buy something through links on our site, Mashable may earn an affiliate commission.

The Apple iPhone 18 Pro is free right now with T-Mobile

On Sunday, Huang told employees he had been "unfaithful to my wife" without naming the person involved...but the New York Times identified her as Alice Huang (no relation), an executive at Nvidia customer Megaspeed, whom U.S. intelligence officials reportedly suspected of spying for China. China denied the allegations, and Nvidia's board, and Donald Trump, said that it had no reason to believe U.S. national security or company secrets had been compromised.

Huang's departure marks the first CEO change in Nvidia's history. The company built its name selling graphics chips for gaming. Those chips went on to become central to training AI models, and under Huang, Nvidia also invested in some of the companies that buy them.

Nvidia shares fell about 10 percent in premarket trading Nov. 12 after the resignation announcement. The company is scheduled to report quarterly earnings on Tuesday, Nov. 17.
