Scandal Forces Jensen Huang Out at Nvidia. Here's How Investors Should Consider Nvidia Stock Now.

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

What makes Nvidia hard to beat doesn’t live in the corner office.

Nvidia (NVDA -7.51%) has spent the past several years establishing itself as the dominant supplier of artificial intelligence processors. Now the company faces a problem that has nothing to do with its chips, its competition, or the enormous demand for AI computing power.

It's a problem of replacing its founder and CEO, Jensen Huang.

Huang resigned Thursday from Nvidia, four days after Nvidia's board of directors opened an independent investigation into reports that a foreign intelligence operation had targeted Huang. The announcement followed Huang's admission that he had been unfaithful to his wife, raising questions about his relationship with a woman suspected of working for Chinese intelligence.

Nvidia stock fell more than 12% in premarket trading on Thursday.

It is an extraordinary development for one of the world's most influential technology executives. And it raises a legitimate question for investors: How should investors evaluate Nvidia stock now that Huang is no longer at the helm?

Jensen Huang has resigned as Nvidia CEO. Image source: Getty Images.

According to reporting by The New York Times and other news organizations, U.S. intelligence officials suspect that Huang had a relationship with Alice Huang, a Chinese businesswoman and former executive at Singapore-based Megaspeed International, who was recruited to gather information for Beijing.

The allegations have not been independently established, and China has rejected them. In addition, The New York Times reported that Huang had a separate affair with a Nvidia employee.

Kress, who is stepping in as interim CEO, has been Nvidia's executive vice president and chief financial officer since she joined the company in September 2013. Nvidia also named its first-ever board chairman, Harvey Jones, a former CEO of Synopsys.

"The board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company's operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption," Jones said.

Back in July, I wrote about the three "Magnificent Seven" companies that remained founder-led: Nvidia, Meta Platforms, and Tesla. I argued that Nvidia was the best long-term investment of the three, largely because of Huang's strategic vision and the competitive advantages his company has built.

Huang co-founded Nvidia in 1993, initially focusing on graphics processors for video games. But one of his most consequential decisions was to create CUDA, a software platform that enabled developers to program Nvidia's graphics processing units for applications far beyond gaming.

That decision helped establish Nvidia's dominance in AI computing.

Today, Nvidia's advantage extends beyond its processors. The combination of the company's networking technology, CUDA platform, and integrated computing systems makes it difficult for its established customers to switch to competing hardware platforms.

Those advantages weren't created overnight, and they won't disappear overnight either.

Huang deserves enormous credit for building Nvidia into the company it is today. But thousands of engineers, researchers, and executives are responsible for developing its products and maintaining its customer relationships.

The company's technological expertise doesn't reside exclusively in the CEO's office.

There's plenty of precedent for successful technology companies prospering after their founders step aside.

Steve Jobs was instrumental in turning Apple into a consumer technology powerhouse. But under the 15-year leadership of Tim Cook, Apple quadrupled its annual revenue, expanded its services business, and became one of the world's most valuable companies. The company is still going strong today with new CEO John Ternus at the helm.

Then there's Microsoft: Bill Gates relinquished the CEO position in 2000, but Microsoft kept moving forward. Under Satya Nadella, who became CEO in 2014, Microsoft became a dominant cloud computing provider and a major force in artificial intelligence.

Jeff Bezos is still synonymous with the company he founded, Amazon, but it has been five years since he handed over the CEO role to Andy Jassy. Under Jassy, Amazon has grown its cloud computing, advertising, and retail businesses, and the company's market cap is approaching $3 trillion.

There's no doubt that Huang's technical expertise, customer relationships, and influence over the company's direction will be difficult to replicate. But a company's long-term success clearly doesn't have to depend on one individual, even when that individual founded it.

The investigations into this situation introduce uncertainty that shareholders shouldn't dismiss. Beyond the change in leadership, any evidence of compromised information or violations of U.S. export restrictions could have serious consequences. But there is currently no established evidence that Nvidia's secrets were compromised.

Meanwhile, demand for AI computing remains the central driver of Nvidia's business. Its customers still need powerful processors, and competitors still face the difficult task of matching Nvidia's vaunted product line.

"Nvidia's strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years," Kress said in a statement released by Nvidia today. "We will continue advancing the work that has made Nvidia a leader in accelerated computing and AI, and execute on the opportunities ahead. Our focus remains on serving our customers, partners, developers and shareholders."

I still think Nvidia is an outstanding company to own for the long term. The board's investigation deserves attention, and its findings could change the assessment of leadership risk.

For now, however, my investment thesis remains intact. Nvidia's greatest asset isn't any one executive, even Jensen Huang. It's the business he helped build, and will continue without him.

Osprey ThrULite Pro 55L Review: Ultralight Comfort From a Backpacking Giant

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Osprey is making a serious push into ultralight backpacking with the new ThrULite Pro series. Long known for its comfortable, feature-rich backpacks, the company is now targeting the ultralight market, long dominated by smaller manufacturers.

The ThrULite Pro comes in 20-, 40-, and 55-liter versions, with the 55L weighing just 1 pound, 11 ounces. That weight is competitive with packs from brands like Hyperlite Mountain Gear, Zpacks, and Gossamer Gear, yet the pack still retains some of the comfort-focused design Osprey is known for.

I tested the ThrULite Pro 55L throughout Maine during late summer and fall, putting in plenty of hiking and guiding miles on rocky trails and arduous mountain terrain. My regular pack is a Hyperlite Mountain Gear Southwest 55, so I was particularly interested in how Osprey’s new offering would compare.

After several months of use, the biggest surprise was just how comfortable this pack is. There are a few design choices I’d change, but Osprey has put together a compelling ultralight option that deserves a closer look.

MSRP: $340* Weight: 27 ounces | 765 grams Materials: “NanoFly” proprietary nylon/UHMWPE ripstop fabric Volume: 20L, 40L, 55L (I reviewed the 55L) Suspension: Minimalist frame with running vest-style harness Closure: Roll-top

I used the ThrULite Pro 55 on hiking and guiding trips throughout Maine in late summer and fall, including long days on rocky, root-covered trails and mountainous terrain. As a Registered Maine Guide, I regularly carry extra equipment for clients, making comfort and accessibility particularly important.

Conditions were mostly dry, with limited rain that provided some opportunity to evaluate the pack’s weather resistance.

One of the most notable features of the ThrULite Pro is its running vest-inspired shoulder harness, a departure from the traditional straps found on most backpacking packs. The design creates a close-fitting, body-hugging carry that distributes weight comfortably across the shoulders and upper body.

Combined with the minimalist suspension system, the result is a pack that feels remarkably comfortable despite weighing less than two pounds. Osprey has eliminated its familiar trampoline-style back panel, cutting weight while maintaining a supportive carrying system with a slimmer foam and mesh panel.

At just 27 ounces, the ThrULite Pro 55 is firmly in ultralight territory. Osprey uses a proprietary lightweight fabric and strips away unnecessary features, including the traditional top lid or “brain,” to keep weight down.

The pack instead uses a roll-top closure, a common feature among ultralight backpacks. While this reduces weight and simplifies the design, I found the closure a bit awkward to use. It also doesn’t allow the pack to cinch down and compress as tightly as I’d like, particularly when carrying smaller loads.

The ThrULite Pro maintains a relatively minimalist layout but offers plenty of exterior storage. The side water bottle pockets are tall and feature adjustable cinch closures at the top, allowing you to tighten them around bottles or other gear for a more secure hold. There’s also a large, deep rear pocket that provides generous storage for items you want to keep separate from the main compartment, such as a rain jacket or other frequently used gear.

The pack also includes two hipbelt pockets, one with a traditional zipper and the other with an elastic closure for quick access. Unfortunately, both are extremely small, limiting their usefulness for snacks, electronics, or other frequently accessed items. It’s an odd design choice considering how practical the rest of the exterior storage is.

Osprey’s proprietary fabric provides a reasonable degree of weather resistance without the weight of heavier traditional pack materials. During my limited rain testing, the fabric shed water effectively, with precipitation beading up and running off the surface.

That said, it didn’t appear to offer quite the same level of protection as Dyneema Composite Fabric (DCF), which is used in many ultralight packs. I didn’t experience prolonged or particularly heavy rain, so I can’t speak to how well the ThrULite Pro would hold up in sustained wet conditions. For longer trips, I’d still recommend using a pack liner to protect critical gear.

Comfort is easily the standout feature of the ThrULite Pro. After plenty of hiking and guiding miles, I found the pack extremely comfortable, with a stable, well-balanced carry that feels more substantial than its 27-ounce weight suggests.

The running vest-style harness and minimalist suspension work well together, providing a secure fit without the bulk of a traditional backpacking suspension system. Even over long days on Maine’s rugged trails, the pack remained comfortable and moved naturally with my body.

Coming from the Hyperlite Southwest 55, I was impressed by how well Osprey managed to combine low weight with the kind of carrying comfort the brand is known for.

Osprey has managed to bring a 55-liter pack below two pounds without making it feel overly stripped down. At 27 ounces, the ThrULite Pro compares favorably with many established ultralight packs.

The minimalist suspension, lightweight fabric, and lack of a traditional brain all contribute to the low weight. While there are lighter options available, the ThrULite Pro strikes a good balance between weight savings and comfort.

I’ve generally preferred minimalist backpacks, and the ThrULite Pro follows that approach. There aren’t a lot of unnecessary features or complicated adjustments, and the overall construction feels straightforward.

Osprey also brings the advantage of its established warranty and repair program, which could make the ThrULite Pro especially appealing to hikers considering their first ultralight pack.

My biggest complaint with the ThrULite Pro is the hipbelt pockets. Both are so small that they’re almost useless for practical storage.

I like having the option of a zippered pocket and an elastic closure, but neither provides enough room for much beyond the smallest essentials. When guiding or hiking long days, I frequently reach for snacks and other small items without stopping, so accessible hipbelt storage is particularly important.

Larger pockets would add minimal weight while making the pack considerably more functional. It’s a frustrating oversight on an otherwise well-designed backpack.

While I appreciate the weight savings from eliminating the traditional top lid, the roll-top system could use some refinement.

I found it somewhat awkward to close, and more importantly, it doesn’t allow the pack to compress as much as I’d like when carrying a smaller load. Compared with my HMG Southwest, which has a straightforward roll-top and side compression system, the Osprey feels less adaptable as gear volume changes.

It’s not a dealbreaker, but it’s something I noticed regularly during testing.

The ThrULite Pro 55L is an impressive entry into the ultralight market from one of backpacking’s biggest manufacturers. At just 27 ounces, Osprey has created a pack that competes with established ultralight offerings while maintaining the comfort that has long been one of the company’s strengths.

After several months of hiking and guiding in Maine, carrying comfort was easily the highlight. The running vest-style harness and minimalist suspension provide an excellent fit, and the pack feels comfortable over long days on rugged terrain.

As previously mentioned, there are a few things I’d modify: the hipbelt pockets are frustratingly small, and the roll-top closure doesn’t compress as effectively as I’d like. I’d also want more time in sustained rain before making any definitive claims about its weather resistance.

Still, these are relatively minor drawbacks compared with how well the pack carries. For hikers looking to move into ultralight backpacking without giving up comfort, the ThrULite Pro 55L is well worth considering.

Hyperlite Mountain Gear Southwest 55 MSRP: $399 Weight: 29.3 oz (Size M) Capacity: 55L

Gossamer Gear Mariposa 60 MSRP: $315 Weight: 34.2 oz Capacity/Max Load: 60L / 35 lb

Zpacks Arc Haul Ultra 60L MSRP: $399 Weight: 22 oz Max Load: 40 lb

The Mandalorian’s New Release Returns To An Abandoned George Lucas Star Wars TV Show

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Six months after The Mandalorian and Grogu brought Pedro Pascal's Din Djarin and his ward Grogu to the big screen, they're back in a new Star Wars release. Since 2019, after the end of the sequel trilogy, we've been in The Mandalorian era of Star Wars. The Lucasfilm TV show was essentially Disney+'s flagship series, and it's so very appropriate that the beloved pair brought the franchise back to the big screen ahead of next year's Starfighter. The Mandalorian and Grogu's box office may have been disappointing, but the film has found new success on Disney+.

Lucasfilm has now published The Art of The Mandalorian and Grogu, a celebration of the series that showcases its development. These art books are always must-buys, and this is no exception; it features stunning double-page spreads of everything from the Hutt homeworld of Nal Hutta to alternate designs for characters and aliens. Most delightfully, The Art of The Mandalorian and Grogu also reveals a George Lucas subplot that was sadly abandoned (but seems to have been incorporated into a tie-in Smugglers' Run mission at Galaxy's Edge).

It seems scripts for The Mandalorian and Grogu initially took the pair to the galactic capital of Coruscant itself, where they'd have confronted Imperial agents on Level 1313. This would have been a very different Coruscant to the city-planet seen in the prequels; the lower levels of Coruscant are a hive of scum and villainy, and it makes sense that the Empire would be active there. Indeed, I'd even suggest this was explicitly set up in The Mandalorian season 3, in a subplot involving Imperial spy Elia Kane.

In the end, this idea was dropped. Jon Favreau felt the New Republic military would handle Imperial unrest on Coruscant themselves, rather than sending a bounty hunter. While I understand his argument, I'm not entirely convinced, given the New Republic did ultimately lose Coruscant to Imperial sympathizers (it's why the city-planet was no longer galactic capital by The Force Awakens). Amusingly, there is still a Coruscant mission in The Mandalorian and Grogu's Smuggler's Run tie-in, so the idea wasn't fully abandoned.

Level 1313 is a matter of Star Wars legend. Back in the early 2000s, George Lucas planned to release the first-ever live-action Star Wars TV show, Underworld, which would have been set on level 1313. This would have explored the galaxy's seedier underbelly, albeit set during the Dark Times of the Empire's reign. Dave Filoni recreated level 1313 in animated form in The Clone Wars season 7, and it seems we almost got this abandoned Lucas concept in The Mandalorian and Grogu as well.

You can buy The Art of The Mandalorian and Grogu here.

Police charge another Tesla driver caught asleep in rush hour as cases pile up

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A Tesla driver caught asleep behind the wheel on Ottawa’s Highway 417 during Tuesday’s rush hour now faces a careless driving charge from the Ontario Provincial Police. The photo OPP shared shows a new-generation Model Y in traffic.

The charge carries a $490 fine and six demerit points on conviction. It’s at least the fifth time this year Canadian police have gone public with a sleeping Tesla driver, and it’s not a coincidence that it’s happening as “Full Self-Driving” gets better.

OPP East Region posted about the stop Wednesday morning. Police didn’t name the brand or the driver-assist system, but the message was clear: “even though your vehicle has an autonomous driving feature, drivers are still required to pay due care and attention when behind the wheel.”

According to OPP, the officer could see the driver’s eyes were closed and hands were off the steering wheel. Instead of lighting up the cruiser, police pulled in close to capture body camera footage first.

Careless driving isn’t a parking ticket in Ontario. The $490 is the set fine, but if the case goes to court, the Highway Traffic Act allows fines up to $2,000, up to six months in jail, and a licence suspension of up to two years.

Ottawa is the latest, but British Columbia has been dealing with this all year:

This isn’t new in Canada either. Back in 2020, Alberta RCMP went further and criminally charged a Tesla driver caught sleeping on Autopilot at 150 km/h with dangerous driving.

The U.S. has the same problem with less enforcement. In September, Colorado State Patrol got a report of a sleeping Tesla driver on I-70 near Georgetown and never found the car. The same month, Rep. Raja Krishnamoorthi pressed the DOT after NBC News found 43 videos of drivers apparently asleep in moving Teslas, 17 of them posted in 2026 alone.

And in October, a driver told CHP he was asleep on FSD when his Tesla drove into a closed crash scene and hit a patrol car in Fairfield, California. Two officers were seriously injured.

A few years ago, FSD needed an intervention every few miles, so nobody was napping. You couldn’t.

Now FSD can handle long highway stretches without a takeover, and that’s exactly when supervision breaks down. A human asked to watch a system that’s right 99% of the time stops watching. That’s automation complacency, and it’s one of the most studied problems in aviation and driving research.

Waymo learned this the hard way. In 2013, Google let employees use its highway driver-assist prototype and caught people falling asleep at highway speed. It decided a system that works most of the time but still needs a human to catch its mistakes was the worst of both worlds, and it went straight to driverless.

Tesla went the other way. It sells a Level 2 system called “Full Self-Driving” that’s good enough to lull people to sleep, and it puts the full responsibility on the human who’s now asleep.

Since FSD v12.4, Tesla has relied mainly on the cabin camera above the rearview mirror to make sure the driver is watching the road. But look at what Tesla’s own owner’s manual says about it: the camera “does not require full visibility of the driver’s eyes in order to monitor attentiveness,” and the system “is still active, for example, if the driver is wearing sunglasses.”

In other words, it’s often judging head position, not whether your eyes are open. When it can’t see your hands, it falls back to asking for a bit of torque on the wheel, which a sleeping driver resting a hand on the rim can provide.

The penalties are soft too. Five “strikeouts” suspend FSD for a week, and each strike is forgiven after three and a half days.

And Tesla has been moving in the wrong direction. Musk said in December 2025 that FSD v14.2.1 lets drivers text “depending on context of surrounding traffic.” In May 2026, he said v14.3.3 “nags less too.” When the car does detect drowsiness, Tesla’s alert reads “Drowsiness detected. Stay focused with FSD.” That pushes a tired driver to lean harder on the system instead of pulling over.

Compare that with GM’s Super Cruise, which uses a steering column camera with infrared LEDs to track head and eye position, and only works hands-free on pre-mapped highways.

The Ottawa driver had their eyes closed and their hands off the wheel in rush hour traffic, and the car was still driving when an officer pulled up beside it. Whatever Tesla’s camera was checking from a foot away, it hadn’t stopped the car before a cop in the next lane did.

We’re glad Canadian police are treating this seriously. A $490 fine and six points is a real deterrent, and OPP’s message is the right one: no matter what Tesla calls it, you’re the driver.

But police can’t ticket their way out of this. These drivers aren’t edge cases anymore. They’re the predictable result of a system that drives well enough to make people relax, sold under a name that tells them relaxing is fine.

The fix is on Tesla’s side, and it isn’t complicated. Detect closed eyes, even behind sunglasses, which is what infrared eye-tracking is built for. When a driver doesn’t respond, slow down and pull over immediately instead of escalating warnings for minutes. Stop telling drowsy drivers to “stay focused with FSD.” And make the strike system actually sting.

NHTSA already forced a recall of over 2 million Teslas in December 2023 over weak Autopilot monitoring. Three years later, people are still sleeping through rush hour on FSD.

Viral Cowboy who took his horse on bus responds after Detroit launches investigation

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A horse owner has responded after a viral video showed them taking their horse onto a bus, and even the city is investigating.

When you’re using public transport, it isn’t a shock to see a dog sitting next to its owner, or other pets being transported in carry cases. These are pretty common sights for regular bus and train riders.

However, on November 9, the internet’s attention was caught by a viral video of two horses being led onto a bus in Detroit. In the clip, which was filmed from across the street, the two riders approached the door while holding their horses on a leash.

Both horses and riders were able to get on board the bus briefly, but they didn’t stick around for long. That left the woman recording the scene, Elessia Drew, in disbelief. “This is crazy,” she said.

The video has been reposted across social media since Drew first posted it to Facebook, attracting millions of views.

Jordan Williams, the horse’s owner, told WXYZ that he helped his horse on board for a funny video, but has taken them on buses before. “We walked on, and I must tell you the buses are a lot smaller than they used to be, or I may have been a little bigger,” he said.

“The point is just to bring light to… like there are cowboys that look like us.”

However, the Detroit Department of Transport has said they are taking the “taking appropriate steps to address this incident” as it is “not consistent with DDOT policy and potentially creates an unsafe and uncomfortable ride for our passengers.”

Williams told WXYZ that he hopes they “look at it like a joke like everyone else,” rather than taking any action.

Americas sex toy shopping habits are not what youd expect

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

I’ve seen enough election maps since last Tuesday. Personally, I’m a little more interested in the one that tells me where people are buying sex toys and which cities prefer birthday cake-flavored lube. Lovehoney’s 2026 U.S. Sex Map (based on 12 months of sales data across 1,216 locations) does exactly that, and I’ve pulled together a few highlights below.

You can also type your city into the interactive tool to see its overall ranking, how it ranks for purchases in categories like vibrators and bondage gear, and its customers’ favorite lube flavor. The tool has two search fields, so you can compare cities side by side. Naturally, I looked up Wilmington, NC (where I currently live). It ranks 171st out of 1,216 locations, and strawberry is the favorite lube flavor. Interesting...def keeping that info on the back burner.

Before anyone starts making assumptions about their neighbors, though, this is shopping data, not a survey of Americans’ sex lives. It tells us what customers bought from one retailer — not how often they have sex, how satisfied they are, or who they voted for.

AdultFriendFinder — readers’ pick for casual connections

Tinder — top pick for fast hookups

Hinge — popular choice for regular meetups

Pure — best for anonymous hookups

OkCupid — best app for inclusivity

Boston takes first place in Lovehoney’s overall city rankings (by product purchases), followed by Somerville, Massachusetts; Reston, Virginia; Bozeman, Montana; and Chapel Hill, North Carolina. The rest of the top 10 includes Blacksburg, Virginia; Royal Oak, Michigan; Fairbanks, Alaska; Boulder, Colorado; and State College, Pennsylvania.

Massachusetts also shows up multiple times in the category awards. Somerville wins for dildo purchases, Boston for strap-ons, and Cambridge for both male sex toys and condoms. All four nominees in the dildo category (Boston, Brookline, Cambridge, and Somerville) are in Massachusetts. You know what, good for them.

Charleston ranks 143rd overall, while Las Vegas comes in at 891st. This surprised me. I would’ve expected a place called Sin City to rank a little higher, but once I thought about it, Vegas does have more sex toy shops than Charleston, so maybe people are just buying local. Again, good for them.

Strawberry is the most popular lubricant flavor nationally, followed by bubblegum, cherry, peach, and birthday cake. At the city level, bubblegum is Wichita's favorite, while Des Moines prefers birthday cake. Strawberry is a fave in Atlanta, Charleston, Charlotte, Columbus, Las Vegas, and Houston.

I’m not sure what I expected from a regional breakdown of lubricant flavors, but Des Moines choosing birthday cake wasn’t it.

Vermont takes Lovehoney’s “Sexiest State” title (the one that buys the most products of all), but it also has the smallest average dildo length in the 2026 state table, at 6.56 inches. Montana has the largest average at 6.98 inches, compared with the reported national average of 6.71 inches. No state’s average exceeds seven inches this year.

Arlington, Virginia, wins Lovehoney’s bondage-purchasing category, while Fairfax takes the lingerie title and Reston leads for nipple clamps. Reston also ranks third overall among cities. Virginia, it turns out, appears in several very different parts of the shopping report.

There's a lot more in the map than I can reasonably get into here, so go ahead and look up your city. Then your hometown. Then wherever your ex moved to. Just remember that a shopping cart doesn’t tell you everything about a person, let alone an entire city.

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Of all the epic conclusions in 2026, from Prime Video's The Boys to HBO's Emmy-favorite Hacks, perhaps the most satisfying was one final outing in Starz's hit series, Outlander. Based on Diana Gabaldon's book series of the same name, this blend of time travel, sci-fi, fantasy, and romance had viewers hooked for 12 years, growing from strength to strength each season as more became invested in the lives of Caitríona Balfe's (Belfast, Ford v Ferrari) Claire and Sam Heughan's (The Spy Who Dumped Me, Love Again) Jamie.

"The final season resembles more of a warm, reassuring hug for viewers," wrote Collider's Carly Lane in her review, one of many gushing with praise for Outlander's last mainline move. Six months on from the Outlander Season 8 finale, has the mind behind this enthralling fantasy tale managed to complete her final installment? Sadly, the answer is no, with there still being no confirmed release date for A Blessing for a Warrior Going Out. There is hope that a release date might arrive in 2027, but nothing is confirmed, and with a nearly-5-year gap between the last book and today, it's important not to get our hopes up just yet.

Zero Hour for PS5, Xbox Series launches December 3

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The PlayStation 5 and Xbox Series versions of tactical first-person shooter Zero Hour will launch on December 3 for $24.99, developer AttritoM7 Productions announced.

Zero Hour first launched in Early Access for PC via Steam on August 12, 2020, followed by a full release on September 9, 2024. It is also available via Epic Games Store.

The console versions will feature full cross-play support, as well as include six premium Weapon Aesthetics Packs—Gold, Platinum, Titanium, Redline, Serpentine, and Savanna—at no additional cost.

“Zero Hour has come a long way since we first released it in Early Access back in 2020, and then the full release four years later,” said Zero Hour director Meheraj Maruf in a press release. “Seeing our game finally make its way to PlayStation and Xbox honestly feels a bit unreal. It’s something we could only dream of when we first started. We have a lot to thank our community for, especially those who have supported us throughout the years and continue to play the game to this day. We wouldn’t have reached this point without them. A huge thanks to Skymap for helping us make this possible, and I really hope everyone has a great time with the game.”

Here is an overview of the game, via AttritoM7 Productions:

Jump in and experience true online tactical gameplay. We want to thank our community for the tremendous support and patience everyone has shown. Join our discord and get closer to the community and the game’s development. We hope you enjoy the game!

Welcome to Zero Hour, a tactical first-person shooter with online team-based action gameplay that is set across various locations in Bangladesh with real-life scale and resource management. Join forces with up to 10 players in intense cooperative missions, or go solo with up to nine AI squad mates. Experience hostage rescue and bomb defusal missions that require calculated strategies in the dynamic five-versus-five player-versus-player mode. Shape your route through compelling situations by interacting with story-driven NPCs and engaging with various objectives.

Engage in intense five-versus-five player-versus-player battles across various game modes, including bomb defusal, hostage rescue, and deathmatch. Experience competitive gameplay with a comprehensive leaderboard and tier system. Choose your loadout wisely, as limited guns and gadgets add a strategic layer—you’ll lose them upon dying, making every decision critical.

Zero Hour presents serious slow-paced, and methodically intense gameplay. The game offers authentic gunplay and scenarios, where quick thinking and strategic execution are essential for victory. Utilize the planning table to overview operations and formulate your approach before taking action. Remember, every move counts, and success demands teamwork, strategy, and precision.

Innovative Gameplay Elements and Weapon Customization

Explore over 18 diverse maps set across various locations in Bangladesh, featuring unique elements like functional elevators, bulletproof glass, and dynamic shutters for strategic advantages. Strategically utilize the breaker switch to control building lights, adding tactical depth to your missions.

Choose from over 23 distinct weapons and 18 utilities, emphasizing resource management and team coordination. Fine-tune your arsenal with detailed weapon customization, modifying rails, sights, and foregrips for optimal performance. Immerse yourself in realism by checking magazine bullet counts and repacking magazines for efficiency.

Doors are your lifeline and your barrier. Infiltrate with caution as terrorists set traps and alarms. Meanwhile, attackers have an arsenal of over 4 breach options: kick, door rammer, C2 explosives, and back kick combined with grenades, ensuring a variety of strategies to navigate these vital entry points.

Face a variety of suspects, each with distinct behaviors. Some even wield suicide bombs, while others utilize sniper tactics in long-range maps. They even have the ability to sense incoming fire and strategically time their advances ensuring maximum safety. The dynamic nature of suspects keeps you on your toes, with no guarantee of their next move.

The K9 Attack Dog feature brings an immersive and emotional experience to your missions. As the game starts, you’re assigned as a dog handler, initiating your journey with a small, nameable puppy. Initially resting in a cage, the puppy soon becomes interactive and starts playing around, following the player through the training facility. After successfully completing three missions, the canine companion becomes available for mission deployment.

Who Is The New CEO Of Nvidia?

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Writer by choice, CryptoCurrency Writer, and Researcher by chance. Currently, focusing on financial news and analysis, as well as cryptocurrency news and data. One may not call me a crypto “Enthusiast” but trust me I'm getting there.

Colette Kress is Nvidia’s new chief executive on an interim basis, after co-founder Jensen Huang resigned with immediate effect on Thursday. Kress has been Nvidia’s chief financial officer since September 2013 and will keep that role during the transition. She guided the company’s finances through its rise into the world’s leading AI chipmaker and is a familiar face to investors from earnings calls. Before Nvidia, she spent about 25 years in finance roles at major tech firms, including Cisco and Microsoft. Kress becomes the first person other than Huang to lead Nvidia since its founding in 1993. The company also named former Synopsys CEO Harvey C. Jones as its first board chair. The board is searching for a permanent CEO and will look at internal and external candidates, with reports naming Apple hardware chief Johny Srouji among possible choices. Kress said she will build on the foundation Huang created over 33 years. Her first major test is Nvidia’s earnings report on November 17.

Pochettino must clean house at Man Utd following Carrick exit, says Neville

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Syndicated reporting provided by Stats Perform and reviewed by the SoccerNews editorial team.

Mauricio Pochettino must completely tear down Manchester United's squad and ensure he has total control of recruitment if he succeeds Michael Carrick, according to Gary Neville.

On Thursday, United confirmed Carrick had been relieved of his duties, less than six months after he was appointed as the Red Devils' head coach on a permanent basis.

It is the latest embarrassing managerial U-turn by Jim Ratcliffe and the INEOS regime, who also sacked Erik ten Hag in October 2024, just four months after handing him a new contract.

With Ruben Amorim also having a miserable stint at Old Trafford, United are now looking to appoint their fourth permanent boss since INEOS took control of football operations at the club in February 2024.

United's tally of eight points after 10 games has them rooted in the Premier League's relegation zone, and even when assuming three-points-for-a-win all-time, they have only had worse starts at this stage in three other campaigns in their history (zero points in 1930-31, six in 1892-93, seven in 1972-73, also eight in 1960-61 and 1986-87).

That miserable position has not appeared to put Pochettino off, with the former Tottenham, Paris Saint-Germain and Chelsea coach reportedly set to leave his role as the United States' head coach to join a club he has long been linked with.

But United legend Neville has warned the Argentine that a complete rebuild of United's squad is necessary if he is to fare any better than his predecessors.

"I think the mistake Mauricio could make is that he comes in and gets an initial bounce, maybe even a six-month bounce [and keeps the players]," Neville told Sky Sports.

"But I remember seeing him at Tottenham, in the early days, and within the first six to eight months in the job, he actually had a whiteboard in his office with all the players' names.

"He'd moved a group of players over to the left-hand side of the board and said, 'these are the ones I need to get rid of, then these are the ones I know I can build around'.

"He's got to identify that very quickly at United, and I'm sure he will. He's more experienced now than he was at Tottenham, but he has a big job to do in terms of players exiting.

Attacking football  Promotes youth  Experience transforming a PL club Mauricio Pochettino ticks a lot of the boxes for a club like Manchester United. But it has also been a long time since he did anything of note in club football. It's an exciting appointment, but one plagued with risks for everyone involved.  https://t.co/B284QkXj6P

— Opta Analyst (@OptaAnalyst) November 12, 2026

"Manchester United have been really poor at getting rid of players in the last 10 years. But they're going to have to invest, and I hope Pochettino has done a couple of things on the way in – I hope he's protected himself with his contract, and I'm sure he's stated that he needs a massive transfer budget.

"I think he needs two defenders straight away, to get Manchester United anywhere near where they want to be this season. And the other thing is, he has to make sure he takes charge of recruitment."

Carrick initially had a hugely positive impact upon replacing Amorim on an interim basis in January.

He led United to a third-place finish in the Premier League last season, winning 12 of his 17 games at the helm in 2025-26 (70%, D3 L2). The Red Devils also averaged 1.94 goals per game in that span, while conceding 1.05.

But looking back, Neville regrets the fact Carrick was appointed on a permanent basis, saying he always feared things might end this way for his former team-mate.

2 – Today is only the sixth time in Premier League history two managers – Michael Carrick and Pierre Sage – have left their positions on the same day during a season, and first since 15th December 2024 (O'Neil at Wolves, Martin at Southampton). Farewells. https://t.co/qi4ojXo0az

He added: "I'm sad that it's come to this. I think I've said this quite a lot in the last 10 years when Manchester United managers have been moved on because it means, obviously, that the club are not being successful on the pitch.

"So there's sadness, more sadness this time, probably, because it's Michael.

"I wish he hadn't got the job at the end of the season. I think at that time, I pretty much said that I felt this could happen. I didn't expect it to happen this quickly, but it is what it is.

"The position in the league is terrible, obviously being in the bottom three, in the relegation zone, and it was inevitable that the decision had to be made."

United's first game back from the international break is a huge meeting with rivals Liverpool at Anfield, on November 22.

Disney's $823M Fantasy Epic With A Sequel On The Way Crosses Massive Streaming Milestone

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Disney's fantasy epic that made $823 million at the box office and has a sequel in development has officially crossed a massive streaming milestone. Disney's fantasy movies are often relegated to the world of animation, with plenty of high-profile stories that have made massive amounts of money at the box office. This includes both Frozen movies, the Moana films, and 2021's Encanto, which each have very different approaches to their respective worlds. But there's also Pixar, which reigns supreme with some of its massively successful fantasy films.

One of its biggest success stories over the last decade has been Coco. The 2017 animated musical fantasy movie follows a 12-year-old boy named Miguel in Mexico, who accidentally finds himself in the Land of the Dead after sneaking into a mausoleum and strumming his musical idol's old guitar. He ends up crossing paths with the spirit of his great-great-grandfather, attempting to get his help in order to return to the world of the living, while also trying to reverse a familial ban on music instilled when he was young.

Now, nine years after the movie was first released in theaters, Coco has returned to the public consciousness thanks to its latest milestone on streaming. According to data from November 12, 2026, Coco has crossed 50 days on Disney+ in the United States. The Pixar movie currently sits at Number 9 on the platform in the US, while also retaining a Number 5 spot worldwide. It's clear that, even long after the movie was originally released, the film still resonates with a wide audience around the world.

It's not too surprising, either, considering reviews for Coco have been overwhelmingly positive from both critics and audiences alike. Its critical acclaim stems from the movie's beautiful animation, strong voice acting, catchy music, and an emotional story about family that resonates with audiences of all ages. The film also has a clear, deep respect for Mexican culture that comes out in its story. These elements have aided in its success, and have justified why it's becoming Pixar's next franchise.

In March 2025, Disney's then-CEO Bob Iger confirmed that Coco 2 was in development. It was later confirmed the movie will be released on November 21, 2029, 12 years after the first movie came out. While no plot details have been confirmed as of writing, it has been reported that Benjamin Bratt will be reprising his role as Ernesto, Miguel's idol whom he meets in the Land of the Dead. Even without any aspect of the story getting confirmed, the movie already sounds like a promising follow-up.

Since Coco is still doing well on streaming almost a decade after its theatrical release, it seems like the movie has what it takes to remain popular until the sequel arrives. The film has retained a major streak in the US and a massive presence around the world, confirming there's always going to be an audience for it. And, when it comes to the Pixar film's quality, that audience is tapped in on what makes the movie such an important part of its history.

NVIDIA CEO resigns amid infidelity scandal, Chinese spy accusations

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

There's a lot going on in tech these days, eh/

NVIDIA CEO Jensen Huang has resigned effective immediately amid concerns about several extramarital affairs, including one with someone who’s suspected to be a Chinese spy.

An investigation is ongoing when it comes to the latter, but for now, Huang has stepped down and been replaced by Colette Kress, NVIDIA’s current executive vice president and chief financial officer in an interim basis.

“NVIDIA’s strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years,” said Kress in a statement. “We will continue advancing the work that has made NVIDIA a leader in accelerated computing and AI, and execute on the opportunities ahead. Our focus remains on serving our customers, partners, developers and shareholders.”

Unsurprisingly, NVIDIA stock dropped about 23 percent this morning as concerns about the company’s stability expectedly linger, on top of a massive plunge in AI stocks last week in the wake of the US elections.

“Colette has helped lead NVIDIA through a period of extraordinary growth and has a deep understanding of our business, customers, operations and financial position,” said Harvey Jones, Chair of the NVIDIA Board of Directors, of the interim CEO. “She is supported by an experienced leadership team that has been instrumental in executing NVIDIA’s strategy and advancing its technology roadmap. The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company’s operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption.”

How will this affect gamers? It’s unclear for now, but the balance of power in the tech industry could shift towards other companies powering the AI bubble like AMD and OpenAI. Either way, pardon my lack of optimism that this is going to change anything or make the price of GPUs suddenly drop to an affordable rate.

Why Scarlett Johansson's Newest Role Proved To Be One Of Her Most Challenging Yet

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: The newest Scarlett Johansson movie presented new challenges for the prolific star.

Johansson is a two-time Academy Award nominee, having been recognized for her performances in Jojo Rabbit and Marriage Story. Along with these accolades, she has the second-highest box office of all time for any actor at $15.6 billion, with the many Marvel Cinematic Universe movies featuring her as Black Widow/Natasha Romanoff being key contributors to that total, in addition to 2016's Jungle Book, Jurassic World Rebirth, Sing, and Sing 2.

In an interview with ScreenRant's Tatiana Hullender for Ray Gunn, Johansson was asked about the experience of voicing pop star Venus Nova, who has many different sides to her, in the new animated movie. The Marriage Story star explained that one of the challenges was that there were sometimes months between voice recording sessions, making it difficult to pick up where she left off while playing the complex character created by director and writer Brad Bird. Check out Johansson and Bird's comments below:

Scarlett Johansson: It's a bit of a journey because when you're doing voice recording, it's over a long period of time. It could be months, actually, before I hear from Brad that he needs me again. And when you come back to it, you may have a different perspective on it, or he may have a new perspective on some version of it because he's trying out stuff, like, "Actually, this is better when it's more playful. We had done it in a more sarcastic way, but it's reading as mean, so let's try this." You get a long time to live in it, and you get better at it too.

The character is very complex, and there's so much to her. We find her in a moment of deep personal crisis. That's when we're introduced to her, and she goes on this very moving journey of self-discovery throughout the film, and self-realization. One thing I will say is challenging about coming back to it is that you have to remember, "Where was I?" I would sometimes ask, "Can you play me what I recorded before?" You have to remember where your voice was at.

Brad Bird: Because it's different sides of the character, and the different sides sound different.

Since Venus is a pop star, Johansson not only had to voice the many different sides of the character, but also sing them too. She was asked whether this was different or more challenging from her previous musical experience, which include the movies Sing, Sing 2, and The Jungle Book.

Scarlett Johansson: It was hard. We had a finite amount of time to do it, and we had a lot of work to do. When we meet her, the character starts out churning out these bubblegum, earworm pop and dance hits, and it very much felt like she was just servicing the audience.

Brad Bird: And yet you can't stop thinking about them!

Scarlett Johansson: Yeah, they were stuck in my head forever! But then she's singing a real confession of her true feelings and her desires, and she's finally singing from her truest self. I luckily had Kimbra there to help me find all of that stuff, because she's obviously a brilliant musical mind, and a very sensitive artist, so she understood all of that. Actually, this character is a collaboration of us; we were working on it together, and her own empathy for the character's situation is all baked into it.

Ray Gunn takes place in an alternate future, in the city of Metropia, where Venus is implicated in a murder, with the accusation threatening to destroy her career. Sam Rockwell voices the titular character, a private investigator hired by Venus' husband, Arnold (Bobby Cannavale), to spy on her, which becomes more complicated as he develops romantic feelings for the pop star. Other key characters are Ray's alien detective partner, Eye-ra (Tom Waits), and alien mob boss Francis "Slugsy" Xavier (Matt Berry).

While Bird is best known for directing and writing Pixar movies like The Incredibles, Ratatouille, and Incredibles 2, he has had the idea for Ray Gunn since the 1990s, and it was inspired by noir films such as The Maltese Falcon, Chinatown, and The Big Lebowski.

The movie was produced by Skydance Animation and will be distributed by Netflix, beginning with a limited theatrical release (including on 70mm screens) on December 4 before coming to the streaming platform on December 18. Bird directed the new film, developed the story, co-wrote the screenplay with Matthew Robbins, and was one of the producers.

After premiering at the London Film Festival, reviews for Ray Gunn have been overwhelmingly positive, leading to a "Certified Fresh" 94% critics' score based on 54 reviews as of this writing. ScreenRant's Ben Gibbons writes that the movie "builds a fascinating world, with deeply thought-provoking themes and intriguing characters."

Outside of Ray Gunn, other Scarlett Johansson movies on the way are James Gray's Paper Tiger, Mike Flanagan's The Exorcist: Martyrs, Matt Reeves' The Batman: Part II, and Ari Aster's Scapegoat. She is playing a homicide detective named Julie Miller in the next installment of The Exorcist franchise, but her roles in The Batman: Part II and Scapegoat remain under wraps.

Ray Gunn releases in theaters on December 4 and on Netflix on December 18.

Michael Carrick sacked LIVE: Man Utd part ways with manager and move to complete Pochettino deal

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The former United midfielder was let go today after the club’s worst start to a domestic season in 40 years

Michael Carrick has been sacked as Manchester United manager after overseeing the club’s worst start to the Premier League season in 40 years, with Mauricio Pochettino looking set to replace him.

A second Old Trafford comeback of the week, this time battling from two goals down twice to rescue a 4-4 draw with Aston Villa on Sunday, was not enough to save Carrick’s job and the club announced on Thursday that it had parted ways with him after just 10 games of the season and with his side 18th during the international break.

United ended a nine-game winless run when they came from two goals down to beat Roma in the Champions League last week but they have not won in eight attempts in the Premier League and have just one top-flight win this campaign.

Carrick survived crisis talks with the United hierarchy after defeats to Como and Bournemouth last month and was given time to turn results around, but speculation over a change begun the day after Sunday’s chaotic draw and The Independent understands contact was initially made with United States boss Pochettino over succeeding Carrick on Monday.

The Old Trafford hierarchy, including Jason Wilcox, Omar Berrarda and Sir Jim Ratcliffe, met in Monaco to discuss Carrick’s future and the club are on the verge of hashing out a deal with Pochettino, who is reportedly ready to accept the position.

Follow latest updates from Old Trafford here:

Paul Scholes thinks Man United might be going in for Mauricio Pochettino a few years too late.

He said on The Good, The Bad & The Football: “I love Pochettino and we work with him at Soccer Aid, didn’t we, he’s a he’s a great man. I think he’s got the presence to manage a big football club like Manchester United.

“He’s got the respect to do it. He works with young players which obviously Manchester United want. My only slight concern is have that we come a little bit too late to the party.

“I think we’ve done that in the past. You look at Van Gaal, his best years were probably behind him. Mourinho again, I think his best years were probably behind him. Saying that, I’m not ruling out what he’s going to do with this Real Madrid team because this Real Madrid team look good.

“I just - when you really think about when we was talking about this two or three weeks ago after the Bournemouth game I don’t think any of us mentioned Pochettino.”

The Manchester United Supporters’ Trust (MUST) has branded Michael Carrick’s sacking “another depressing moment” and argued the club’s problems rest primarily with its ownership, in a now-familiar plea to end the ongoing feeling of crisis at Old Trafford.

United sacked Carrick just 10 games into the Premier League season with the club in the relegation zone and having won just once in this domestic campaign.

The club’s hierarchy met minority owner Jim Ratcliffe in Monaco on Wednesday to discuss Carrick’s future and a statement on Thursday said that it had “come to the extremely difficult conclusion that a change is necessary in order to achieve our ambitions”.

Today is only the sixth time in Premier League history two managers - Michael Carrick and Pierre Sage - have left their positions on the same day during a season.

The last time it happened actually wasn’t that long ago, with Wolves boss Gary O'Neil and Southampton manager Russell Martin both getting canned on 15 December 2024.

Pochettino is still contracted to US Soccer until the 2030 World Cup though an early departure from his deal feels increasingly likely. United have made contact with US Soccer over paying compensation to release Pochettino with rumoured figures reaching as high a $10m (£7.4m).

Should an agreement be made, Pochettino could, in theory, leave the US before the scheduled games against Haiti in order to spend time with Man United’s squad ahead of the crucial clash at Anfield.

In that case he could arrive at Carrington either the same day or a day after being formally announced as the new head coach. That would give him a maximum of nine days to prepare the team if his appointment is confirmed today (Thursday 12 November).

The more likelier option is that Pochettino leads the USMNT for their two fixtures with Haiti and reports to Carrington shortly afterwards.

In that case, he could be in post at Man United as early as Wednesday 18 November though given the time difference between the US and the UK, Pochettino would arrive late in the afternoon or early evening.

Even so, that would give him three full days, Thursday 19, Friday 20 and Saturday 21 to settle into his new environment and have time on the grass with the squad.

Thoughts have now turned to Michael Carrick’s replacement with former Tottenham, Chelsea and Paris Saint-Germain manager Mauricio Pochettino the odds on favourite to take over.

Pochettino is the current manager of the United States Men’s National Team (USMNT) but has reportedly reached a verbal agreement with Man United to sign of as their new head coach. Should his appointment be confirmed when will Pochettino officially begin his tenure?

Man United’s next match comes on Sunday 22 November when they travel to Anfield to take on Liverpool. It will arguably be the most difficult start possible for whoever takes over and it is a game United need to take something from or else they risk dropping further into the relegation zone.

The current international break would usually provide a new manager with time to train and see the squad in action but Pochettino, should he be given the job, is unlikely to have such a luxury.

That is because the United States have two Nations League matches to play on November 14 and 18 during this window and Pochettino is likely to oversee both fixtures.

Gary Neville has branded Manchester United ‘classless’ over how they handled the dismissal of Michael Carrick.

United finally pulled the trigger on Carrick’s sacking after days of leaving their manager in limbo, despite it becoming near-common knowledge that the club had been speaking to Mauricio Pochettino in an effort to acquire him as his replacement.

Speaking on the Overlap’s Stick to Football podcast, which was recorded prior to Thursday’s confirmation, he said: “We’re sat here today recording this programme knowing that Michael Carrick is going to be sacked tomorrow, knowing that they’re speaking to Mauricio Pochettino, knowing that they’ve spoken to (Julian) Nagelsmann, (Eddie) Howe – and they should be doing that by the way, I’m not saying they shouldn’t be doing that…

“But I think the fact they’re in Monaco now and they can’t sack him today knowing that it’s happening, to me it just feels a little bit clumsy, it feels a little bit classless.

“I think if you live in Monaco, you can’t leave a guy hanging for 24 or 48 hours just because you live in Monaco, that can’t happen.”

Manchester United are closing in on a deal to appoint Mauricio Pochettino before their next game against Liverpool following Michael Carrick’s sacking.

United must strike a deal with US Soccer, with a solution required over the United States’ next two international games in particular, while the club’s hierarchy is meeting at a Monaco summit to finalise a decision over the club’s manager.

Compensation, worth an initial $7m (£5.3m), will be a formality due to the clause in Pochettino’s existing contract, that runs until the 2030 World Cup.

But one major hurdle remains to ensure Pochettino can arrive in time for the trip to Anfield.

Rio Ferdinand has shown his support for former Manchester United team-mate Michael Carrick after his sacking.

“Forever legend!” he posted on X. “Showed dignity and class as always.”

He later criticised the club’s official statement confirming his exit, posting: “Man United statement like ChatGPT... I wanna hear a voice!”

Manchester United’s penchant for implosion during the first half of the season, often after a summer of intense financial backing, is quite evident from this stat.

The club’s last five permanent managers have all been sacked between 28 October and 5 January.

It’s almost as if recruitment and management are not aligned...

Crystal Palace have sacked manager Pierre Sage after the club slumped to bottom of the Premier League table for the first time since December 2017.

The Frenchman moved to Selhurst Park on a three-year contract this summer after an impressive year at Lens, where he led the club to a runner-up finish in Ligue 1, won the Coupe de France and Trophee des Champions titles for the first time, and qualified for the Champions League.

But he was unable to replicate that success across his 16 games in all competitions at Palace, who have conceded the most goals of any top-flight side this season and claimed just seven points out of a possible 30 during his tenure, and sit two points off safety going into the November international break.

How 4 finance executives rose, and how many S&P 500 CFOs became CEOs

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Free daily briefing on global business news.

The CFO to CEO path, traced through careers at Oracle, TikTok, Hertz and Deutsche Bank, plus data on how many S&P 500 CFOs who switched jobs became CEOs

Russell Reynolds Associates found that 33% of S&P 500 chief financial officers who changed jobs in 2022 moved into chief executive roles. In 2021, that share was 8.8%. Those two figures frame this slideshow: the move from CFO to CEO, and the executives whose careers run alongside it.

The finance job itself has widened. The role has moved well beyond number crunching. Today's CFO analyzes data to spot trends, weighs risks and plans for growth. Finance chiefs also often drive digital transformation, the effort to rebuild how a company runs around newer technology.

The careers in this selection trace that move in different ways. Safra Catz has been an Oracle $ORCL executive since April 1999. She served as co-president and CFO starting in 2011 and later became the company's sole CEO. Shou Zi Chew was CFO at Xiaomi and then at ByteDance before taking over as TikTok's chief executive. Stephen M. Scheer started at Goldman Sachs $GS in 1993, held a CFO role from 2018 and became CEO of Hertz in February 2022.

Not every path here ends in the top job. James von Moltke became Deutsche Bank's CFO in 2017. He has also served as the bank's president since March 2022 while keeping the finance role. At TikTok, the stretch between Kevin Mayer's exit after a few months as CEO and Chew's appointment was covered by Vanessa Pappas. She was the company's General Manager, not its CFO, and served as interim global head.

The slides that follow trace these careers one at a time. They then step back to the wider pattern. That includes the share of S&P 500 finance chiefs moving into chief executive roles and the analytical and technology work that now sits with the CFO. It also covers the banking backgrounds several of these executives share. Goldman Sachs appears in the early careers of both Chew and Scheer. Von Moltke started at Credit Suisse First Boston in London in 1992 and spent eight years at Citigroup $C. Catz's route ran instead through a long tenure inside Oracle.

Safra Catz reached the top of Oracle $ORCL by moving up through the company's own ranks. She joined as an executive in April 1999 and later served as co-president and chief financial officer. She then shared the chief executive role with Mark Hurd before becoming Oracle's sole CEO.

Her climb took place inside a single company. Catz was not an outside hire brought in to run Oracle.

A board seat came early. Oracle added Catz to its board of directors in 2001, about two years after she started as an executive there. She had a place in the company's governance before she held the president title.

In 2004, she was named president. Her board appointment had come three years earlier.

The finance role followed in 2011, when Catz became co-president and CFO. In that position, she reported to Larry Ellison, Oracle's cofounder. The post put her in charge of the company's finances while she kept a share of the president's title.

The order of those appointments sets her career apart from a straight finance track. Seven years separated her appointment as president from her move into the finance job. She did not rise through finance first and then move into general management. The CFO post came after she had already reached a senior leadership title.

The route to chief executive opened when Ellison stepped down as CEO. Catz did not take the post alone at first. She became co-CEO alongside Mark Hurd, and the two split the top job.

She later became Oracle's sole CEO. That ended the period in which the top job was divided between two executives.

Her profile extends beyond Oracle. Fortune has listed her among the most powerful women in business. She has also sat on Disney $DIS's board since 2018. That seat gives her a governance role at a second large U.S. company.

Shou Zi Chew became TikTok's CEO after serving as chief financial officer at two technology companies, Xiaomi and ByteDance. He took over the role after Kevin Mayer quit as TikTok's chief executive.

Chew began his career in banking. He spent two years as a banker at Goldman Sachs $GS in London.

He then moved to the investing side. Chew worked at venture firms, including DST Global.

During that stretch he led investments in JD.com and Xiaomi. Those deals put him on the investor side of both companies before he held a job inside either one.

One of those investments became his next employer. Chew was named Xiaomi's CFO in 2015. The appointment moved him from backing the smartphone maker to running its finances.

His second finance chief role came at ByteDance, TikTok's parent company. Chew became ByteDance's CFO in March 2021.

That post placed him inside the company that owns TikTok. When he later became TikTok's chief executive, he moved from the parent company's finance office to the head of its subsidiary.

The opening came when Mayer left the CEO post after a few months in the role. Vanessa Pappas, TikTok's General Manager, served as interim global head until Chew took over.

Each stage of Chew's career connects to the one before it. A banking start led to venture investing. An investment in Xiaomi led to a finance job there. A finance job at ByteDance led to the top job at TikTok.

He held the CFO title at two different companies before he ran one.

Vanessa Pappas, TikTok's General Manager, served as the company's interim global head after Kevin Mayer quit as CEO. She held that position until Shou Zi Chew took over the top job. She is the only interim leader among the executives profiled here.

Mayer's stint as chief executive was brief. He left after only a few months. Pappas then led the company during the gap before a permanent CEO was named.

An interim chief's job is to keep the company running through a leadership transition. At TikTok, that role went to an executive who already worked at the company.

Pappas did not come to the job through finance. Her title at TikTok was General Manager, not chief financial officer. Every other executive profiled in this selection held a CFO title at some point. That makes her the exception in a list built around finance leaders. Her case shows that a temporary top post does not have to go to the finance chief.

Pappas is Australian. She graduated from the University of Queensland in Brisbane. Before joining TikTok, she worked at Next New Networks and at YouTube. Both companies are online video businesses. That background sits in the same field as TikTok, an app built around short videos.

She also commented on the app's commercial direction. In an interview with CNBC, Pappas said she saw ways Walmart $WMT could work together with the e-commerce features newly added to the TikTok app. Walmart is a U.S. retail chain.

The permanent job went to Chew, whose career had run through finance roles. His appointment ended the interim period that Pappas had overseen.

The TikTok handover ran from Mayer to Pappas to Chew. The temporary leader in that sequence came from outside the finance function. The finance executive arrived afterward and took the CEO title.

Stephen M. Scheer became CEO of Hertz in February 2022 after a career spent in finance. He also holds a seat on the Hertz board. That places him on the company's governing body as well as at the head of its management.

His career started in 1993 in the Financial Institutions Group at Goldman Sachs $GS. At an investment bank, a financial institutions group advises banks, insurers, asset managers and other financial companies on mergers, acquisitions and capital raising. That work centers on the balance sheets and regulation of lenders and other financial firms.

Scheer moved into a chief financial officer role in 2018. He held that post in 2019, the year Goldman Sachs and Apple $AAPL launched Apple Card. The card took a Wall Street bank into consumer credit cards in partnership with a consumer technology company.

The Hertz appointment put a longtime finance executive in charge of a car rental company. A rental business of that kind buys large numbers of vehicles, finances them, rents them out and later sells them. Fleet funding and used-car values sit close to the core of its economics.

Outside Hertz, Scheer sits on the boards of the Jewish Museum and the New York Stem Cell Foundation. The Jewish Museum is an art and culture museum on Fifth Avenue in Manhattan. The New York Stem Cell Foundation is a nonprofit that funds and conducts stem cell research.

He is also a member of the Business Roundtable, the Council on Foreign Relations and the U.S. Travel and Tourism Advisory Board. The Business Roundtable is an association of chief executives of major U.S. companies. The Council on Foreign Relations is a New York-based membership organization and think tank focused on foreign policy. The travel advisory board counsels the U.S. secretary of commerce on policy affecting travel and tourism. That industry is the market Hertz serves.

James von Moltke has been Deutsche Bank's chief financial officer since 2017, and since March 2022 he has also served as the bank's president. He kept the finance job when he took the second title. He now holds both posts at once.

That sets his case apart from the others in this selection. Von Moltke is not Deutsche Bank's CEO. His career shows a finance chief whose remit widened inside one company. It does not show a move from the CFO seat into the chief executive's chair.

His banking career spans more than 30 years. It began in 1992 at Credit Suisse First Boston in London. He later spent eight years at Citigroup $C. He became Deutsche Bank's finance chief in 2017. That put 25 years of banking between his first job and his arrival in the bank's CFO role. Deutsche Bank is the third bank named in that record.

The president title came about five years into his tenure as CFO. A Deutsche Bank release describing changes to the responsibilities of its management board stated that von Moltke was to remain chief financial officer.

His responsibilities grew again in 2023. Since that year he has overseen Asset Management. That adds a business division to a portfolio that already combined the CFO and president roles.

Read in sequence, the changes follow one line. Von Moltke became CFO in 2017, added the president's title in March 2022 and took on Asset Management in 2023. Each change happened at the same bank. None of them involved the chief executive's job.

His titles are president and CFO. That places him outside the CFO to CEO path followed by Safra Catz at Oracle $ORCL, Shou Zi Chew at TikTok and Stephen M. Scheer at Hertz. Catz, Chew and Scheer each reached the chief executive's office. Von Moltke widened his remit without leaving the finance post.

In 2022, 33% of S&P 500 chief financial officers who changed jobs became chief executives, according to Russell Reynolds Associates. In 2021, the share was 8.8%.

That is a rise of 24.2 percentage points in one year. The 2022 share was about 3.75 times the 2021 level.

Put in plainer terms, roughly one in three job-changing finance chiefs landed the top post in 2022. A year earlier, fewer than one in 10 did.

The figure covers a defined group. It counts S&P 500 CFOs who moved to a new position during the year, not every finance chief in the index. A CFO who stayed in the same role through 2022 is outside the calculation.

Both numbers are shares, not head counts. The data does not state how many CFOs changed jobs in either year. A percentage can rise because more finance chiefs became CEOs, because fewer changed jobs overall, or both. The figures alone cannot separate those effects.

The measure records one step in a career. It shows whether a departing finance chief's next job was the top executive post. The two percentages do not say whether those CEO roles were permanent or interim. They also say nothing about how the new chief executives performed after taking over.

This limits what the figures can support about stand-in leaders. The data cannot show how often a company names its own CFO as an interim CEO. It also does not record whether a new CEO post came at the same company or at a different one. Safra Catz followed the same-company route at Oracle $ORCL, where she was CFO before becoming CEO.

The data spans two years, 2021 and 2022. It describes a one-year jump among job-changing CFOs at the large U.S. public companies in the S&P 500, not a longer trend. The figures carry no breakdown by sector and no stated cause for the jump.

The modern CFO's job reaches beyond bookkeeping. Rydoo describes finance chiefs who study company data to find trends, assess risks and map out growth, not only produce the numbers.

Number crunching remains part of the work. In Rydoo's account, it is no longer the whole of it.

Each of the three tasks Rydoo names covers a different part of the job. Spotting trends means reading data for patterns that matter to the business. Weighing risk means judging what could go wrong and how much exposure a company can carry. Planning growth means deciding where the business should put its money and effort next.

Rydoo sets out this wider scope in its CFO Corner piece on finance leaders who moved into chief executive roles.

Finance chiefs often drive digital transformation inside their companies, according to Rydoo's CFO Corner. The publication lists that work among the responsibilities that now sit with the CFO.

Rydoo presents digital transformation as one sign of how far the finance job has broadened.

The publication makes the point in its coverage of finance leaders who went on to become chief executives.

Shou Zi Chew, Stephen M. Scheer and James von Moltke all began their careers at large investment banks before reaching finance leadership. Chew and Scheer started at Goldman Sachs $GS. Von Moltke started at Credit Suisse First Boston.

Scheer's first job, in 1993, was in the Financial Institutions Group at Goldman Sachs. Von Moltke began a year earlier, in 1992, at Credit Suisse First Boston in London. He went on to spend eight years at Citigroup $C. In total, he has more than 30 years in banking.

The two reached the finance chief's office on nearly matching timelines. Von Moltke became Deutsche Bank's CFO in 2017. Scheer held a CFO post from 2018. In both cases, 25 years separated the first banking job from the CFO title.

Chew's banking stint was much shorter. He spent two years as a Goldman Sachs banker in London. He then moved into venture investing at firms including DST Global. There he led investments in JD.com and Xiaomi. In 2015 he became CFO of Xiaomi, a company he had backed as an investor. His route to finance leadership ran through deal-making rather than a long run inside a bank.

The banking start did not lead to a single outcome. Chew moved on to the CFO post at ByteDance in March 2021. He later took over as TikTok's CEO after Kevin Mayer quit. Scheer became CEO of Hertz in February 2022. Von Moltke has stayed in finance at Deutsche Bank. He added the president's title in March 2022 and oversight of Asset Management in 2023 while keeping the CFO role. He does not hold the CEO post.

Catz's route is measured in years at one company. She has been an Oracle $ORCL executive since April 1999. She became co-president and CFO in 2011, 12 years later. After Larry Ellison stepped down as CEO, she shared the top job with Mark Hurd. She later held it alone.

Across these four careers, two routes to the CFO office appear. One starts at a bank and then moves through other firms. The other runs through long tenure inside a single company, with the finance role serving as one step toward the chief executive's office.
