How Beginners Can Build Cycling Endurance in Just 3 Days a Week

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

This coach-backed base training plan helps newer riders improve aerobic fitness, add structure, and make progress with limited time.

Building a base on the bike has traditionally meant putting in a lot of hours doing long, slow distance training. But with work and family lives, it can feel impossible to find the hours in the week to properly commit to a base build, especially if you’re a beginner cyclist who’s not used to rides that take more than three hours.

As it turns out, especially if you’re a newer cyclist, there’s a lot that you can accomplish if you only have three days a week to get on the bike. Building a base of fitness for cycling takes some commitment and consistency, but it doesn’t have to take over your life.

Here, we spoke with Namrita Brooke, Ph.D., one of the lead coaches at BaseCamp, about how she would structure the optimal three-day week on the bike—and what other lifestyle tweaks she would make in order to improve fitness even when you can’t fit in long rides.

A lot of cyclists think that if they can’t train 10-plus hours per week, their fitness will suffer, or they won’t make gains on the bike. This isn’t true, especially for newer riders. “Someone who has not been doing any exercise would see gains in as little as two to four hours a week of riding,” says Brooke. “If someone has some general fitness, gains could be had in four to six hours [per week].”

However, expect that after a few weeks or months, the tangible signs of progress will slow unless you can devote more time to training. “The tricky part is the ongoing gains might not be as easy to see as the initial gains,” Brooke adds. “Initially, if the athlete was measuring power, they would see power increases, but these would plateau in a few months, leading them to think gains are no longer occurring but in reality, the athlete is likely to still build other metrics of fitness like repeatability, durability, and resilience, but those are not as easy to measure.”

Often, new cyclists with limited time tend to attempt one monster ride on the weekend, skipping cycling during the week due to busy lives. But even if you can only eke out two one-hour (or 45-minute!) trainer sessions on weekdays in addition to the long weekend ride, you’ll gain fitness and strength faster than if you stick to a single ride.

“I would rather see the athlete do three quality days a week versus one or even two longer days,” says Brooke.

“The base training goal, whether you’re a pro or average Joe, is to build the best aerobic fitness you can for the training hours you have available,” says Brooke. Without this aerobic fitness, you won’t be able to boost your speed or power on the bike.

This means spending the bulk of your time on the bike in zone 2 (whether you use power, heart rate or perceived exertion). Riding in zone 2—also known as your endurance pace—will mean that you’re able to hold a conversation and speak in full sentences, but it should feel slightly difficult to do so. If you can chat away easily, you’re riding a little too easy. If you’re gasping for air between sentences, you’re riding a little too hard.

By that, we mean your skin. Riding more miles means being aware of skin considerations such as sun protection and chafing prevention. Don’t just ignore reddened skin, use lubricants and padded shorts to feel more comfortable on the bike.

For newer riders, opt for the longer durations in each phase. If you’ve been riding for a while, you’ll likely notice that after a few weeks in each phase, you’re at a point where you’re not noticing any improvements—in that case, proceed to the next phase. Here’s how Brooke would break it down.

2.5-3.5 hours (zone 2), based on how much free time you have available

New riders may need to break up the zone 3 portion of the ride with an easy 5-10 minutes in the middle.

2.5-4 hour (zone 2) based on how much free time you have available

Have an extra hour on Saturday for your long ride? Great! Woke up an hour early on Wednesday and have time to hop on the trainer for an extra 45-minute ride in zone 2? Fantastic. Feel free to add time whenever possible.

Four days on the bike is better than three, says Brooke. “Two harder interval days and two longer days would be best if possible,” she notes. And this is actually a fairly standard schedule for most serious cyclists—they’ll add one or two additional easy rides to the schedule, but the meat and potatoes of their training consists of two longer endurance rides and two harder interval-based sessions.

In fact, Brooke says that often, she hears new cyclists say they only have four hours per week to train, but once they get going, they start to “find” extra time. “You would be surprised how many newer riders think they are limited to four to six hours a week but once they really get into cycling, they make accommodations to squeeze in an extra hour or two per week,” she says. “I see riders all the time start to wake up earlier to squeeze in additional ride time before they start the rest of the day!”

And thanks to the prevalence of indoor trainers, it’s easier than ever to sneak in an extra hour or two on the bike during a weekend or early in the morning, even if you have work or childcare demands on your time. But don’t neglect outdoor riding, especially if your base-building goal is to be able to keep up on the local group ride or jump into a race. While indoor training builds fitness, it doesn’t help improve your skills for riding outdoors, so make time for at least one outdoor ride per week if at all possible.

There is one final caveat: It’s easy to get excited about the prospect of adding a monster ride to your weekend whenever possible, but these long rides aren’t easy. Four hours on the bike will feel very, very different from two hours. If riding for five hours on Saturday means you can barely walk for two days after, or it impacts your training the next week, back off.

Long rides shouldn’t leave you flattened for days afterwards. If they do, you’re doing too much, too soon. And Brooke notes that the longer you ride, the more important it is to properly fuel that long effort!

The fastest way to improve your fitness is—again—by consistently training. But training doesn’t mean all-bike-all-the-time! Brooke says there are quite a few simple additions you can do on those non-cycling days to see gains on the bike.

→ Walk: Often on conference calls for work, or accompanying your kiddos to soccer practice? Walk and talk, or do laps around the field while you cheer. “Walking is more of an aerobic builder than people think and can be a gentle form of aerobic activity to accompany bike time,” Brooke says.

For the office worker, treadmill desks are becoming more popular, and if you do want to put in more bike time and you have a stationary trainer, you can do some easy spinning while answering emails or brainstorming for that big presentation.

→ Strength Train: “Strength training can be added in as little as 15 minutes a day, three days a week if we are willing to change the paradigm of going to the gym and lifting heavy things,” says Brooke. “Integrating just this small amount of functional strength into a base training program like we do at BaseCamp yields long-term benefits to your training and health.”

You can do shortstrength sessions at home with just a couple of weights, or even bodyweight alone to get started.

In a typical training plan, cyclists have three weeks of training followed by a rest week where they do only a couple of shorter, easier rides. When you’re only able to ride three days a week, there’s no reason to take a rest week—you’re already getting plenty of recovery time in the four days you’re off the bike.

“The low training stimulus is likely not enough to elicit a high enough super-compensation response to make the loss of fitness during a rest week worth it,” says Brooke. (That said, if you’re a beginner rider and find that after three weeks, you are feeling fatigued, you can dial down the duration or intensity for that fourth week—but stick to three rides if possible.)

Once you’ve done your base training for several weeks with your rides spread out through the week (i.e Tuesday, Thursday, and Saturday), you may want to try switching things up. If you do have the ability to try out new riding days, it’s worth experimenting with doing mini-training blocks, i.e riding three days in a row, some weeks. This subtle shift changes your training stimulus by forcing your body to contend with the need for speedier recovery between rides.

Newer riders may find that it’s harder to recover between rides in these chunks, says Brooke. “The challenge of block training is the athlete needs a certain amount of base fitness to be able to respond and adapt to this type of stimuli and in this scenario,” she explains. “But testing the block process is the only way to find out how they tolerate it.”

If you try riding three days in a row and feel exhausted, go back to your regular schedule and test again after another few weeks of consistent training.

While it’s tempting to stay in the base phase forever, this training phase should really only last around eight weeks. At this point, your aerobic fitness is maximized (for now) and it’s time to focus on the development of “power.”

“After the base phase comes the build phase, where the athlete wants to increase their sustainable max power for a range of times,” says Brooke. “For a newer cyclist without a specific goal, focus on holding a higher wattage for five minutes to 40 minutes. This phase should be maintained as long as the athlete continues to see improvement, then once response begins to fade, move on to the next phases.”

Once you’ve plateaued your power gains, the final phase, typically utilized when “peaking” for an event would be the “speed” phase, explains Brooke. “Here, the cyclist would focus on improving shorter efforts of under five minutes and building anaerobic capacity to focus on performing better in events or group rides.”

After your main event, whether it’s a race or group ride or even just spending four to six weeks focusing on upping your sprint power, it’s time to take a short break and then circle back to base phase.

The good news is that once you’ve completed this cycle, you’ll be pleasantly surprised at how much stronger you’re feeling on the bike the second time you tackle base training. While training phases are cyclical, your fitness grows as you spend more and more time on the bike, so no training phase will feel exactly the same.

Molly writes about cycling, nutrition and training with an emphasis on bringing more women into sport. She's the author of nine books including the Shred Girls series and is the founder of Strong Girl Publishing. She co-hosts The Consummate Athlete Podcast and spends most of her free time biking and running on trails, occasionally joined by her mini-dachshund.

Coach David Lipscomb is the founder of CIS Training Systems, creator of THE SYSTEM 6×6 Framework, and a 1st-degree Black Belt who's spent over three decades helping cyclists of all levels transform their performance through data, discipline, and purpose.

Steve Carell, John C. Reilly to Star in Neil Simon’s ‘The Odd Couple’ on Broadway

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Steve Carell and John C. Reilly will star in the Broadway revival of Neil Simon’s classic The Odd Couple. Helmed by Tony Award-winning director Thomas Kail (Hamilton), the revival opens on March 23 at the Stephen Sondheim Theatre.

Marking what would have been the 100th birthday of playwright Simon, his seminal play first opened on Broadway in 1965 and starred Walter Matthau and Art Carney. The original production won four Tonys, including Best Play. It also spawned the 1968 film and five-season-long popular television series of the same name, as well as countless subsequent productions worldwide.

“Neil Simon wrote one of the great American comedies, and it is an honor and a thrill to be part of this exceptional team bringing this play back to Broadway,” Kail said in a statement. “Underneath the extraordinary humor is something deeply human. Steve and John are two remarkable actors who can make an audience laugh uproariously one moment and move them deeply in the next. We aim to create a world that feels alive, immediate, and can clearly express why this play has endured for more than 60 years.”

“Why are we doing this? We need to laugh. Period,” one of the show’s producers, Jeffrey Seller, added.

General tickets go on sale beginning Tuesday, Nov. 17 at 10 a.m. ET via the show’s site. Carell, who made his Broadway debut in 2024’s Uncle Vanya where he portrayed the titular role, will play Felix Unger. The actor has starred across the small and big screen, including in the films The 40-Year-Old Virgin, Little Miss Sunshine, The Big Short, Beautiful Boy, Vice, Battle of the Sexes, Crazy, Stupid, Love, and the Despicable Me franchise. He won a Golden Globe for Best Actor – Television Series Musical or Comedy in 2006 for his role as Michael Scott in The Office.

Reilly will portray Oscar Madison. His Broadway credits include starring in Sam Shepard’s True West opposite Philip Seymour Hoffman, playing Stanley Kowalski in A Streetcar Named Desire, and starring in Steppenwolf Theatre Company’s The Grapes of Wrath. He has also appeared in numerous films, including Boogie Nights, Magnolia, Gangs of New York, The Hours, The Aviator, Talladega Nights: The Ballad of Ricky Bobby, Step Brothers, Walk Hard: The Dewey Cox Story, The Sisters Brothers, and Stan & Ollie. In 2025, he released What’s Not to Love?, his debut album under his vaudevillian persona, Mister Romantic.

DTCC plans to bring tokenized securities to Solana in second half of 2027

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Looking for institutional-grade crypto trading? Discover LMAX Digital, the regulated exchange from LMAX Group

The planned Solana integration follows the launch of DTCC's tokenization service on Canton Network and its private Besu network last month.

The Depository Trust & Clearing Corporation announced Thursday it plans to bring tokenized versions of eligible securities held by its Depository Trust Company subsidiary to Solana Solana (SOL) in the second half of 2027, connecting its DTCC Tokenization Service to the Layer 1 blockchain.

The service went live on Oct. 28, initially on Canton Network and DTCC's private Besu network. Eligible users can convert their securities into tokenized versions and back again, with delivery to digital wallets of their choice. DTCC said at launch that it planned to add further blockchain networks next year.

"DTC-tokenized assets made available on Solana – and all blockchain networks integrated with the service – will retain the same investor protections, ownership rights and regulatory safeguards that apply to traditionally held securities," Brian Steele, president of DTCC Clearing & Securities Services, said in the announcement.

DTC received a Securities and Exchange Commission no-action letter last December for the tokenization service, allowing DTC to tokenize Russell 1000 stocks, major U.S. equity index exchange-traded funds, and U.S. Treasury bills, bonds, and notes.

DTCC's first limited production trades of tokenized stocks and Treasurys began in July on Canton and Besu, with JPMorgan tokenizing part of its Invesco QQQ Trust holdings held at DTCC in the trial. The bank retained the ability to convert them back to traditional shares.

DTCC named 20 participants that had signed agreements to use the service at its October launch, including Bank of America, Citi, JPMorgan, Morgan Stanley, and UBS. DTCC said its depository subsidiary provided custody and asset servicing for $114 trillion in securities in 2025.

Jensen Huang resigns as Nvidia chief executive

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Company accepted resignation yesterday, it reveals in filing

Nvidia's Jensen Huang has resigned as CEO of the company, following the revelation of his affair with a woman suspected of being a Chinese spy and reports of a relationhip another woman who was a company employee.

In a Form 8-K [PDF] filed with the US Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC), the GPU maker disclosed that it accepted the resignation of Huang, whom it identified as Jen-Hsun Huang, on November 11, effective immediately.

He is being replaced by chief financial officer Colette Kress, who will serve as interim leader while the board conducts a search for a new CEO.

Huang, who founded Nvidia in 1993 and served as president, CEO, and a member of the board of directors ever since, led the GPU maker to become one of the most valuable companies in the world, hitting a hit peak of over $5.5 trillion earlier this year.

His position became untenable following an investigation by the company's board after he admitted to an affair, amid allegations he had a romantic relationship with a woman the US authorities suspected as a Chinese spy.

Reports later surfaced this week of a second alleged affair with an employee of the company itself, reported by the New York Times as being with Kimberly Powell, Nvidia's vice president of health care.

Kaylee Hottle’s Sister Will Step in to Complete ‘Godzilla’ Actor’s Last Movie After Her Death at 18 Years Old

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Kayle Hottle’s sister will step in to complete the actor’s final film role following her death.

The “Godzilla vs. Kong” star died earlier this year in a car accident. She was 18 years old.

Before her death, Hottle had planned to star in “What Doesn’t Kill Us,” a feature film about people with disabilities who find that what the world might label a limitation is actually an incredible strength. Related Stories Charli xcx Joins Martin Scorsese's 'What Happens at Night' With Leonardo DiCaprio and Jennifer Lawrence (EXCLUSIVE)

The film’s cast, which is made up of actors with disabilities, includes Alaqua Cox (“Hawkeye”) and Maddie Haining.

Hottle was deaf and came from a multi-generational deaf family. Her sister Jasmine will now step in to complete the movie.

“Like Kaylee, Jasmine is deaf, and her involvement allows us to honor Kaylee’s memory while continuing the work she was going to be part of,” Andy Mingo, who wrote and produces the film, told People magazine.

“Kaylee’s passing was an unimaginable tragedy, and her loss is felt deeply by everyone involved with the project. When we began discussing how to move forward, the possibility of Jasmine taking on her sister’s role emerged as something particularly meaningful.”

“For us, Jasmine stepping into Kaylee’s role isn’t simply a casting replacement,” he continued. “It’s an opportunity to preserve something of Kaylee’s legacy while telling a story that we hope will resonate with audiences far beyond the disability community.”

Before her Hottle had combined her studies at the Texas School for the Deaf, where she competed on the track-and-field team, with roles in blockbusters such as “Godzilla vs. Kong” and “Godzilla x Kong: The New Empire.” She played Jia in the film series, the adoptive daughter of Dr. Ilene Andrews (Rebecca Hall), earning a Saturn Award nomination for best performance by a younger actor.

Hottle, who was fluent in American Sign Language, got her start in commercials promoting support for the deaf community. She also appeared in a 2021 episode of “Magnum P.I.”

Neighbor Review – Rent Out a Garage or Driveway, but Insure It Yourself

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Many or all of the credit card, banking, and other offers on this site are from companies that pay us. For example, we may be paid when you click a link, are approved for a product, or open an account. Compensation may influence which products we cover and how, where, and in what order they appear. It doesn’t influence our editorial evaluations, ratings, or opinions, which are ours alone. We don’t include every bank, credit card company, or offer available in the marketplace.

Rent out an unused garage, driveway, or lot on Neighbor if it would sit empty for months, since listing is free and the fee is 4.9% plus 30 cents a payout. Get your landlord’s or homeowners association’s OK first, and ask your insurer whether your policy still covers the space, because Neighbor’s guarantee doesn’t cover damage to your property.

Neighbor lets you rent out an unused garage, driveway, shed, or lot to people who need storage or parking, at a monthly price you set. Garages list for about $330 a month in Seattle but about $100 in Des Moines, where a driveway spot lists for as little as $37.

A garage or driveway that would otherwise sit empty for months earns its host $94.80 of every $100 in rent, with Neighbor billing the renter. If you already know someone who needs the space, renting to them on a written agreement saves the fee, but you’d collect the rent yourself without the guarantee or payout protection.

Finland Turns Bitcoin Miners Into Discounted Home-Heating

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Home » Bitcoin (BTC) » Finland Turns Bitcoin Miners Into Discounted Home-Heating

In the town of Nokia, Finland, a 180-square-meter house now relies entirely on Bitcoin mining rigs for warmth.

During the frigid months of winter in Northern Europe, heating efficiency is the main topic of discussion even in the richest neighborhoods. The frequently hostile surroundings have urged heating specialists to come up with new, creative ways to replace traditional oil heating with cutting-edge technology.

That’s where Bitcoin (BTC) comes into play: Finnish homes have already adapted liquid-cooled mining machine heaters. They utilize the gains equipped from Bitcoin (BTC) mining venues to offset the costs of heating. On the other hand, that kind of solution is highly-dependent on the hash-rate difficulty across the network, among factors like general market conditions.

The next BTC block-reward halving comes in 2028, which could arguably cap the profits of this kind of business if Bitcoin (BTC) crosses $135,000. With installation costs exceeding $20,000, SunBit’s Bitcoin Boiler is aimed at relieving high-heating costs for the residents of Finland. The machine is supposed to work in-profit as long as Bitcoin’s price stays above $70,000.

Some people use Bitcoin mining to make money.In Finland, people are using it to heat their homes.A Finnish startup called SunBit has installed Bitcoin mining machines that double as home heating systems.The concept is pretty wild.Instead of letting the heat generated by… pic.twitter.com/9S4hqTYSl6

For instance, in a historical house in Tampere, Finland’s third-largest city, mining rigs replaced wood burning, maintaining a 1,500-liter water tank between 50°C and 60°C. Sunbit’s liquid-cooled Bitcoin miner utilizes the Antminer S21 XP Hydro, employing a hash rate of up to 495T. The device consumes 5.3 kilowatts of power & draws about 46,400 kWh annually.

The Bitcoin Boiler device stores this heat in water, reaching temperatures up to 60 degrees Celsius, which is then circulated throughout the house via radiators and underfloor heating pipes. It costs roughly $5,200 a year to run. SunBit founder Elmeri Suomi stated: “The heating function itself is unaffected by market conditions; only the payback period changes.”

🇫🇮 🔥 Underground Bitcoin mining in Finland is used to heat its towns, instead of being wasted. pic.twitter.com/EHOfxcWq6M

Bitcoin-based heating systems have first been tested in the Netherlands, but Finland’s adoption mark has grown way further. The Bitcoin Boiler service fee is optional and includes 24/7 remote monitoring with automatic heating strategy optimization aligned with Finland’s floating electricity prices. This runs in a companion app with grid peak-shaving options for advanced enthusiasts.

BBC Boss Challenged By Staff Over Culture Of “Reverence” Towards Powerful Stars

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

EXCLUSIVE: BBC director-general Matt Brittin has argued that the entertainment business is lagging behind other industries in tackling workplace power abuses amid the Russell Brand and David Walliams scandals.

During an internal town hall meeting on Thursday, Brittin was challenged by staff over a culture of “reverence” towards on-screen stars. The former Google executive admitted that decision-making processes around talent can be “subjective,” while he also called on the BBC to be more transparent in complaints handling.

During the staff call, the most upvoted staff question read: “The behaviour of people like Russell Brand, Gregg Wallace and David Walliams, amongst many others, was widely known yet they continued to work here. How can we ensure that we are protecting people now rather than paying lip service to the behavior of the talent and apologizing in 15 years’ time?” Related Stories News BBC Boss Matt Brittin & Industry Leaders Set To Attend Culture Secretary's Emergency Meeting Next Week Following Brand & Walliams Scandals News 'Line Of Duty': Arnott, Fleming & Hastings Return To Service In First Look At Season 7 Of BBC's Record-Setting Cop Drama

Another BBC employee added: “The revelations about Russell Brand and David Walliams point to a culture of ‘reverence’ towards ‘talent.’ What will change to make sure that production staff feel like they do have a voice and that they will be supported by more senior management?” Watch on Deadline

Brittin commended the “brave” journalists and survivors who helped bring Brand to justice after he was convicted of rape and sexual assault. He echoed this sentiment on Walliams, though qualified that the allegations of predatory behavior reported by ITV News and The Guardian were “unproven.” Walliams has strongly denied wrongdoing.

“I want to just call out that at the heart of this is people being prepared to come forward,” he said. “I do think there’s change afoot in the world.” But Brittin added that the entertainment industry needs to get better at holding people to account as they move between different productions.

He explained: “Personally, I think the entertainment and media industry is behind others in terms of standards. I think one of the reasons is we’re in an industry where there are subjective decisions about talent. You can’t make those decisions objectively. We’re often in a program structure that isn’t continuing over years, with strong HR and other processes around it.”

The BBC conducted a workplace culture review last year, which found that there were a minority of employees who were “able to behave unacceptably without it being addressed.” Since then, Brittin said the BBC has made progress in resolving complaints quickly, while more employees feel able to raise concerns. He added that work needs to be done on preventing power abuses.

“What we want to do is have a culture where these behaviors, or even micro versions of behaviors that are not acceptable, are nipped in the bud early,” Brittin said. He added that there needs to be greater transparency for complainants during grievance processes.

“If there’s a complaint, often the complainant doesn’t hear back, and sometimes complaints are not upheld. So you might be upset about something, but there isn’t a finding. So we need to be clear about those things,” he said. “And obviously, some go through to being investigated, and there are some with consequences, and some of those consequences lead to termination or other action against employees. So we need to sort of look at how we can be more transparent.”

Culture secretary Lisa Nandy has convened an emergency meeting about industry safety next week. Nandy will tell TV leaders that if their response to the recent allegations is not robust enough, she may consider introducing legislation to force them to fund the just-launched CIISA anonymous whistleblowing service.

“I want to be clear that if protections are insufficient, the government will not hesitate to take further action,” Nandy said. “For too long, too many people have had to choose between their careers and safety. People must be safe in their jobs, whistleblowers must be protected and those who abuse their position must no longer be able to hide.”

Despite the circumstances, Nvidia thanks Huang "for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry"

When you purchase through links on our site, we may earn an affiliate commission. Here’s how it works.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang has resigned "effective immediately," the company has announced following allegations of the leader having an affair with a suspected Chinese spy – and further reports of a second affair surfacing just yesterday.

In a press release shared today, Nvidia confirms that chief financial officer and executive vice president Colette Kress will serve as interim CEO while the company's board searches for a permanent replacement for Huang.

Despite the circumstances, Nvidia still speaks highly of Huang in the statement. "Jensen's contributions to NVIDIA are profound," it begins. "NVIDIA is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. The Board and the company thank Jensen for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry."

Huang admitted to having an affair earlier this week, but the situation was even more complicated than it could have been due to allegations that the person he was having that affair with might be a Chinese spy. Nvidia launched an investigation, with Huang acknowledging the "national security concerns" and saying he will "cooperate fully." He added: "I love our country and our company. I am committed to protecting NVIDIA and supporting America's national security. I am proud and grateful to be an American."

Beyond that, more recent reports alleged that Huang was also engaged in a second affair with one of his employees – specifically, Nvidia's vice president of healthcare, Kimberly Powell. After this, it was claimed that the company's board was meeting to decide whether to oust Huang.

Nvidia's leadership change here is unprecedented – Huang has served as the company's sole CEO since its founding 33 years ago. In addition to Kress taking over in the interim, Nvidia has announced that it has appointed its first chair of the board, Harvey C. Jones, the former CEO of Synopsys.

“NVIDIA's strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years," Kress says. "We will continue advancing the work that has made NVIDIA a leader in accelerated computing and AI, and execute on the opportunities ahead. Our focus remains on serving our customers, partners, developers and shareholders."

The Best Fitness Gifts for People Who Love to Sweat

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

If you buy through our links, we may earn an affiliate commission. This supports our mission to get more people active and outside.Learn about Outside Online's affiliate link policy

Smartwool Trail Run Nature Print Crew Socks

Fitness gear makes a particularly tricky gift because the people who use it most need it to cater to their (often intensely) personal needs. The good stuff works for many varied athletes, disappears during a workout, and keeps annoying little problems from becoming big ones, or makes recovery suck a bit less.

From blister-fighting socks to a $40 bandana that earns its hefty price tag, here are 13 gifts for people who would rather be sweating.

Giving socks for the holidays feels unimaginative—until those socks eliminate the hot spot that usually appears around mile 12. Smartwool uses 53 percent merino wool blended with synthetic fibers for durability and stretch, then reinforces high-wear areas with its Indestructawool construction. The 4 Degree Elite Fit System has targeted stretch zones to keep everything locked in place, while mesh zones improve breathability, and a nearly seamless toe reduces friction, making these absolute blister-mitigating machines. These are the best socks you could give.

The highest compliment I can give a running shirt is that I completely forgot about it on the run. Adidas built the ADI365 Climacool Iconic Tee around that goal, using lightweight performance material and targeted ventilation to move heat and sweat away from your skin during hard efforts. The athletic shape keeps excess fabric under control without squeezing you like a race singlet. At $35, it’s also refreshingly reasonable in a category where shirts now often cost twice that.

I have sacrificed dozens of reusable bottles to protein shakes with smells that became structurally bonded to the plastic and ruined everything poured into them after. RTIC attacks that problem with a ceramic-lined 18/8 stainless-steel interior designed to reduce lingering tastes and odors. Double-wall vacuum insulation keeps drinks cold for up to 24 hours, while a removable shaker helps mix powders. The chug lid gets a magnetic cap that stays out of your face while drinking. The whole setup is built for athletes who want one bottle to handle everything from water to protein shakes.

Forty dollars for a bandana sounds ridiculous until you use it every single day of a two-week climbing trip. Arc’teryx cuts the Sinsolo from ultralight 20-denier recycled nylon and adds breathable mesh sections, creating a one-ounce triangle that dries quickly and takes up basically no room. This makes the Sinsolo a utility player that can be worn as a headband, cover a burning neck from the sun, or be dunked in a creek to make an on-the-go swamp cooler.

Consuming a recovery drink should not feel like the final unpleasant interval of a workout. Tailwind’s mix wins because it goes down easily while still delivering meaningful nutrition with 20 grams of complete plant protein from fava and brown rice sources, enough carbohydrates to replenish glycogen, plus sodium, potassium, calcium, and magnesium. Just as importantly, it actually tastes good mixed with plain water, so recovery doesn’t require dragging out a blender when you’re already cooked.

Ciele helped transform running hats from disposable race swag into something runners happily collect. The GOCap SC earns the brand obsession with lightweight, fast-drying material, strategic ventilation, reflective details, and a pliable brim you can crush into luggage without ruining it. The soft design disappears on long runs and absolutely sucks sweat off foreheads. It also looks good enough that the recipient might keep wearing it after the run instead of immediately stuffing it into the bottom of a gym bag.

A 2.5-inch short leaves very little room for mediocre fabric or poor fit. Vuori uses its smooth BlissBlend Form knit, a 74 percent polyamide and 26 percent elastane blend with four-way stretch and moisture-wicking capability. The high-rise waistband gets subtle V shaping at the front and back, while the short inseam stays completely out of the way during yoga or Pilates. This short is minimal without feeling flimsy, and it’s polished enough to continue wearing after class.

A sports bra shouldn’t require a warmup just to pull over your head. Beyond Yoga’s Glowzone aims for support without sacrificing the incredibly soft, stretchy feel the brand is known for. Medium-impact construction makes it useful for less bouncy activities like studio work and lifting, while the streamlined shape layers easily under a tank. The Electric Fuchsia color injects joy into any workout.

The Moab XC’s large shield provides plenty of coverage, and Tifosi offers lens options designed to maintain effective contrast as light changes. I particularly like the pink-tinted lens for variable trail conditions because it makes terrain pop without turning shady woods into nighttime. The broad coverage also works beautifully on a mountain bike. They’re sport sunglasses, but they don’t make you look like you’re trying to win the Tour.

The Orosi leggings are the most organic-feeling leggings we’ve tested, and they still completely hold their own in the gym. Credit the substantial 10-ounce jersey made of 27 percent hemp, 61 percent organic cotton, and 12 percent spandex. That magic amalgam of fibers creates a softer, more natural hand while preserving enough stretch for yoga and strength work. A triangular gusset improves mobility, and flatlock seams help reduce irritation.

RENPHO’s Active Mini Thermacool shrinks percussion massage into a compact package, and adds hot and cold treatment options for another layer of recovery. Dialing up the heat while hammering tired quads adds a soothing element to an otherwise aggressive massage. It’s small enough that it doesn’t monopolize luggage space, which makes me much more likely to actually bring it when traveling.

Nathan takes a refreshingly straightforward approach to an often overcomplicated category with the AeroZip Hydration Vest. Four-way-stretch perforated fabric hugs the body, a full front zipper makes getting it on simple, and adjustable side cords dial in fit without leaving webbing or bungees flapping everywhere. Two 17-ounce HydraPak flasks ride on the chest, and the vest itself weighs just 4.8 ounces.

From warm up to recovery to everything else the rest of the day brings, this is a staple that every active guy needs in his closet. The soft performance four-way stretch knit that’s moisture-wicking and maximizes comfort all the same while the tapered leg and cuffed ankle create a clean, athletic look. The two inseam options make it a great gift, and the zippered pockets keep belongings secure from the gym to afternoon errands and beyond.

These feel a little ridiculous underfoot because of how well they buoy each stride. The Cloudmonster 3 Hyper combines On’s oversized CloudTec geometry with highly responsive Helion HF PEBA-based foam and a more durable, stable layer beneath it. There’s no rigid plate, so your stride rolls smoothly off the aggressive rocker rather than feeling controlled by mechanical stiffness. At around 9.6 ounces (men’s) and 7.5 ounces (women’s), with a 6-millimeter drop, it’s a maximal shoe that feels refreshingly light and agile underfoot.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang Resigns Amid Alleged Spying Scandal

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

The longtime Nvidia boss will be replaced with the company's Executive Vice president

After a month of headlines about an affair with an alleged Chinese intelligence asset, Nvidia Chief Executive Officer Jensen Huang has stepped down from his leadership position at the AI chip company. Nvidia announced the decision on Thursday morning in a press release that described the company's next steps. Replacing Huang is Colette Kress, the current Executive Vice President and Chief Financial Officer of NVIDIA. Kress will be serving in an interim capacity until Nvidia finds a permanent successor.

Within video games, Nvidia is known as the company that powers computer graphics chips that make PC gaming possible. At large, Nvidia designs and is responsible for the vast majority of computer chips used for artificial intelligence. Nvidia's products are highly in-demand, which has led to an increase in gaming console prices across the board. Thanks to the AI boom, Nvidia and its multi-trillion dollar valuation have made it one of the biggest and most important companies in the world.

Nvidia was originally founded by Jensen in 1993, and the executive has been central to the company's public identity ever since. Back in October 2026, Huang was reported to be at the center of a Chinese spy plot. According to the New York Times, an intelligence asset named Alice Huang developed a close relationship with the Nvidia CEO with the apparent intention of directing more of the company's computer chips to China. The intelligence asset also reportedly sent back information she learned back to China. The tech mogul has since been accused of having an affair with another executive who reported directly to him — an act that violated Nvidia's code of conduct.

Though these are matters involving Huang's personal life, his position in the world of AI — and the government's agenda to strip China of any potential tech advantage — have made the affair a matter of national security. Beyond his position at Nvidia, Huang is considered a close ally of President Donald Trump.

Kress has served as Executive Vice President and Chief Financial Officer of NVIDIA since joining the company in September 2013. During her tenure, she has led the company’s finance organization and has played a key role in NVIDIA’s growth and evolution. Prior to joining NVIDIA, Kress spent nearly 25 years in finance roles at leading technology companies, including Cisco and Microsoft.

The Board of Directors appointed Harvey C. Jones as its first-ever Chair of the Board. Mr. Jones has been an entrepreneur, technology executive, and active venture investor for over 30 years, including serving as Chief Executive Officer and Executive Chairman of Synopsys.

“Colette has helped lead NVIDIA through a period of extraordinary growth and has a deep understanding of our business, customers, operations and financial position,” said Harvey Jones, Chair of the NVIDIA Board of Directors. “She is supported by an experienced leadership team that has been instrumental in executing NVIDIA’s strategy and advancing its technology roadmap. The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company’s operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption.”

“NVIDIA’s strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years,” said Colette Kress, Interim CEO and CFO, NVIDIA. “We will continue advancing the work that has made NVIDIA a leader in accelerated computing and AI, and execute on the opportunities ahead. Our focus remains on serving our customers, partners, developers and shareholders.”

Jensen’s contributions to NVIDIA are profound. NVIDIA is one of the most consequential technology companies in the world. The Board and the company thank Jensen for his immeasurable contributions to the company and the industry.

Jensen Huang has resigned as Nvidia’s chief executive, president and board member, effective immediately, the company confirmed in a statement on Thursday after a late-night board meeting on Wednesday. The move follows reports of two extramarital affairs, one with former Megaspeed executive Alice Huang, whom US intelligence reportedly suspects of spying for China, and another with Nvidia healthcare vice president Kimberly Powell, who reported to him. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO. In a shift in how the company is run, Nvidia also appointed former Synopsys CEO Harvey C. Jones as the first board chair in its history. Nvidia’s statement did not include any comment from Huang. Kress said she would build on the foundation Huang created over 33 years and keep the focus on customers and shareholders. The board has begun searching for a permanent CEO and will consider both internal and external candidates, with Apple hardware chief Johny Srouji among names mentioned in reports.

South Korean Photographers Demand Police Investigation After Politician Calls Them ‘Hyenas’ in Chatroom

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

South Korean Photographers Demand Police Investigation After Politician Calls Them ‘Hyenas’ in Chatroom

A leading South Korean politician is facing fierce backlash from the country’s media after referring to photojournalists as a “pack of hyenas” in an online chatroom — an insult that has prompted a formal police complaint from press photographers.

Representative Choi Min-hee is a South Korean politician and senior member of the ruling Democratic Party of Korea (DPK), serving in the country’s National Assembly. She has sparked outrage in South Korea after sharing an image of working press photographers in a group chat and making disparaging comments about the photojournalists.

Last week, Min-hee uploaded photos of photographers covering DPK events to a KakaoTalk group chat room for local party members in Namyangju, a city in South Korea. In the post uploaded to the chat room, which had over 500 participants, Min-hee referred to the photographers in the image as a “pack of hyenas.”

“These are journalists from media outlets with access to the National Assembly,” Min-hee writes in the post, according to numerous South Korean news outlets. “They’re doing this to capture divisive footage somehow.”

Min-hee’s disparaging post about South Korean photographers quickly spread online, sparking an angry backlash from members of the press. Amid the uproar, she held a press conference at the National Assembly on Wednesday and apologized for her remarks. However, the Korean Photographers Association has nonetheless decided to file a police complaint against Min-hee for disparaging photographers as a “pack of hyenas.”

The Korean Photographers Association says the politician’s remarks demean journalists, undermine legitimate press scrutiny of those in power, and cross the line from acceptable criticism into defamatory accusations.

“Condemning legitimate reporting activities as political divisiveness and ridiculing journalists by comparing them to animals is a clear insult to media professionals and an inappropriate act with potential for defamation,” the Korean Photographers Association writes in a statement to Wednesday, according to national news outlet Cho Sun Daily.

“Photographers do not exist to capture scenes that suit politicians’ preferences. They exist to monitor power through the eyes of the public and record history. Criticism of the media is free, but insults and baseless accusations against reporters cannot be justified.”

The association says the photographers pictured in Min-hee’s post have suffered significant emotional distress and humiliation as a result.

“We have finalized the decision to file a complaint with the police to prevent infringement on our members’ rights,” the Korean Photographers Association adds. “Photographers whose faces were exposed in the photos are currently experiencing severe humiliation and self-loathing, struggling to the point of being unable to continue their work.”

7 Common 5K Training Mistakes Runners Make

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Everything you should avoid for a faster 3.1 miles.

The 5K is a staple in many runners’ racing schedules, and training for one comes with its own unique strategies for interval workouts, long runs, nutrition, and recovery. All of these factors are bound to leave room for error, so you’ll likely run into some pitfalls as you prep for 3.1 miles.

Some common missteps runners face might even lead to injury, explains Maya Ellison, C.P.T., RRCA-certified run coach and owner of P.A.C.E.D. Coaching Services. If you don’t optimize your training method, you’re also likely to keep getting the same results, which may not lead to that PR you’re vying to achieve, she says. Even if you’re a seasoned runner with dozens of 5Ks under your belt, there’s always room for improvement. “Doing it before doesn’t make you an expert,” says Ellison.

So you can surely take a couple notes from run coaches in order to run your best 5K yet. To better prepare for all your favorite seasonal 3.1-milers and help you feel confident at the start line, we spoke with run coaches to unpack the most common mistakes runners make when training for a 5K.

For many runners, 5Ks are easier to jump into compared to a marathon, which requires a solid block of training time to adjust to high mileage and fueling demands. With a 5K, you might tag along at a friend’s race or sign up last minute. Because of this, you might squeeze in a bunch of speed workouts within a few weeks before the race, which can leave you feeling tired or flat come race day, says Sam Renikoff, USATF- and RRCA-certified run coach.

While you can get away with a shorter training block for a 5K, it’s still important to give yourself enough time to absorb your training, recover, and feel 100 percent going into the race, Renikoff explains. For example, rather than tapering for three weeks before race day like you would training for a marathon, Renikoff recommends tapering for a week to 10 days before a 5K, keeping intensity high with speedwork but cutting back on volume.

Plus, going from zero to 100 can lead to getting sidelined, according to Debbie Woodruff, USATF- and RRCA-certified run coach and certified personal trainer. That’s because runners who increase mileage too quickly—rather than taking small steps toward more volume—are more likely to face injury, according to a study published in the British Journal of Sports Medicine.

To mitigate the risk, Woodruff recommends beginners follow a couch-to-5K plan or a run/walk training plan to ease into training with a gradual buildup, while more seasoned runners should follow an eight-week-long plan that also allows you to build mileage, add speedwork, and prioritize the prerace taper.

Sticking to an expert-backed training plan, like Runner’s World’s 5K training plans, allows you to have structure and give yourself time to adjust to the demands of the distance.

Renikoff explains that even though 5Ks are much shorter than a marathon or half, the long run is still crucial. It allows you to get comfortable with being uncomfortable, an important part of the 5K. It also prevents you from giving into fatigue by building your endurance, which allows you to hold onto your pace through the entire 3.1 miles. Plus, the long run often acts as a confidence booster, making the 5K less intimidating all around.

The long run is one of the best ways to increase your aerobic capacity, Renikoff explains. This is important even for shorter race distances because you need to use oxygen to efficiently produce energy for any run lasting longer than just a few minutes.

Long runs during 5K training will typically range anywhere from 4.5 miles (for more beginners) to 12 miles (for advanced runners with time-based goals like breaking 20 minutes). That means some plans may triple the distance of your race, which will only make you more prepared to conquer 3.1 miles.

Seasoned runners in particular might struggle to keep their easy runs easy, Ellison explains, because of their experience, fitness levels, or overall ambition. But these easy days give your body a chance to recover from hard days, allowing you to get stronger throughout your training cycle, she says.

You should feel relaxed throughout your easy runs, like you’re able to have a conversation with someone. Some runners find it helpful to check in on their effort level with the ABC test: Chant the ABCs out loud and try to get to the letter G without having to take a breath. If you falter, that’s your cue that you need to slow down.

If you make a habit of not taking easy pace seriously, you might even find yourself running a speedy first mile during an easy run or tempo run, only to falter by mile two, progressively getting slower and more fatigued by the end. This happens when runners struggle to find an effective pacing strategy, Renikoff explains.

This makes it more likely you’ll go out too fast on race day, which could sabotage your goals. “If you go out and run the first mile [of the race] 20 seconds faster than your average 5K pace, you’re a lot more likely to have that pace balloon to 30 seconds or more slower [than goal pace] in the second and third miles, which is not only not fun or enjoyable, but it’s also not fast,” Renikoff says.

For smarter pacing, Renikoff suggests progression runs: Start by running the first mile of your tempo run at a moderate pace, then increase your speed each mile or half mile (depending on the distance of your run) by about 10 seconds, finishing significantly faster than you started. This requires you to start slow enough that you can continue to run faster as the workout progresses.

While some runners start their runs too fast, others struggle to run faster than their easy pace at all. “If you’re aiming to run a fast 5K and you have not spent any time in those paces by doing some hard intervals, it’s really easy to give up in the race,” says Renikoff.

The key to not giving up at the first sign of fatigue? Implement one to two days per week of some form of speed training, including intervals and tempo runs, Woodruff says.

Seasoned runners may benefit from track intervals, like 200- to 400-meter repeats at 95-percent effort or goal 5K pace with equal recovery, she explains. Another option is a tempo or threshold run, where you sustain your 5K pace for a prolonged period of time sandwiched by a few easy miles.

A fartlek workout introduces newbie runners to interval training, Woodruff explains, by alternating one to two minutes at a hard effort with one to two minutes at an easy effort, essentially practicing interval training without the need for specific metrics.

If your most recent PR is a 24-minute 5K and you’re trying to run your next one in 19 minutes, you might need to readjust your goal setting, at least for now. Ellison explains that runners sometimes set overly ambitious goals, when in reality, true growth takes time.

Instead of looking to drop five minutes in your next race—which requires you to run nearly two minutes faster per mile—try setting a smaller goal like dropping 30 seconds to one minute from your finish time, Ellison explains. This requires about a 10- to 20-second pace difference per mile, making your PR more manageable to achieve.

A beginner 5K runner may not know how to properly warm up and cool down, explains Ellison, which is especially important for each workout, but also for race day.

Warmups can help you sustain a harder effort for longer periods of time, while cooldowns give your body a chance to actively recover after hard efforts. A solid warmup typically includes dynamic stretching, drills like high knees and butt kicks, along with a five-minute jog, Woodruff explains, while a cooldown should include a mile or two of easy jogging and static stretching.

Kristine Kearns, a writer and avid runner, joined Runner’s World and Bicycling in July 2024. She previously coached high school girls cross country and currently competes in seasonal races, with more than six years of distance training and an affinity for weightlifting. You can find her wearing purple, baking cupcakes, and visiting her local farmers market.

Steve Carell & John C. Reilly To Star In ‘The Odd Couple’ Broadway Revival This Spring

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Steve Carell and John C. Reilly will star in a new Broadway production of Neil Simon’s The Odd Couple, with Hamilton director Thomas Kail on board.

The production begins a 15-week limited engagement on March 3, 2027, at the Stephen Sondheim Theatre, with an opening night on Tuesday, March 23.

Joining Carell, who will play the persnickety Felix Ungar, and Reilly, as the sloppy Oscar Madison, will be Laura Benanti as Gwendolyn Pigeon; Kevin Del Aguila as Roy; James Monroe Iglehart as Murray; Rob McClure as Vinnie; Heléne Yorke as Cecily Pigeon; and David Zayas as Speed.

The production is produced by Jeffrey Seller, The Season (Mike Karns and Steven Tartick), Thomas Kail, and Emanuel Azenberg, the longtime producer of Neil Simon’s work. Watch on Deadline

Director and producer Kail said, in a statement, “Neil Simon wrote one of the great American comedies, and it is an honor and a thrill to be part of this exceptional team bringing this play back to Broadway. Underneath the extraordinary humor is something deeply human. Steve and John are two remarkable actors who can make an audience laugh uproariously one moment and move them deeply in the next. We aim to create a world that feels alive, immediate, and can clearly express why this play has endured for more than sixty years.”

The revival marks the centennial of Simon’s birth. The playwright died in 2018.

The creative team includes scenic designer David Korins, costume designer Brenda Abbandandolo, lighting designer Stacey Derosier, and sound designer Jill BC Du Boff.

Originally opening on Broadway in 1965, The Odd Couple was directed by Mike Nichols and starred Walter Matthau and Art Carney. The original production won four Tony Awards and inspired the 1968 film adaptation, the long-running television series starring Tony Randall and Jack Klugman, and numerous productions around the world.

Carell, who rose to fame as the star of The Office, made his Broadway debut in 2024 in the title role of Lincoln Center Theater’s revival of Anton Chekhov’s Uncle Vanya. Reilly, known for such films as Boogie Nights, Walk Hard: The Dewey Cox Story and Steppenwolf Theatre Company’s The Grapes of Wrath.

‘Star Wars: Episode IV – A New Hope’ To Play At Cosm As Disney Teams With Shared Reality Firm

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

A new version of Star Wars: Episode IV – A New Hope will debut at Cosm‘s shared reality venues in March 2027, the franchise kickoff’s 50th anniversary year.

The engagement will kick off a multi-year, multi-film partnership between Disney and Cosm, which also has deals in place with Warner Bros. and Universal. It also received a $100 million investment from Sony Pictures Entertainment last June.

Cosm’s four existing locations, in Inglewood, CA, near SoFi Stadium as well as in Atlanta, Dallas and Detroit, are known for their signature domes equipped with massive, wrap-around 12K screens. When films play at Cosm, the main 16×9 image is surrounded by an array of enhancements and immersive featured designed to deepen the audience experience. Related Stories News Adam Driver Says No More Kylo Ren: 'Star Wars' "Ship Has Sailed" News 'Star Wars' Voices & Sounds Made Available To Gamers And Creators By Tech Firm Voicemod

The upcoming Cosm shared reality version of A New Hope, based on a 2019 update of the sci-fi classic. Representatives from Disney, Lucasfilm, Industrial Light & Magic, and MakeMake have collaborated with Cosm on the project. The 50th anniversary of the film will also a run in various Imax formats, along with a major merchandise blitz. Watch on Deadline

In keeping with momentum being experienced by Imax, Sphere and other experiential companies, Cosm has found strong demand from audiences willing to pay a premium for unique, high-end entertainment. The privately held company was founded in 2020 and opened its L.A. flagship three years later, announcing $250 million in funding the following year.

Its backers include casino magnate Steve Winn, former Milwaukee Bucks co-owner Marc Lasry, Cleveland Cavaliers owner Dan Gilbert, and Blackstone partner and sports team investor David Blitzer. Live sports broadcasts, which feature dynamic pricing and the ability for Cosm to seat additional guests in areas outside the main dome, are fueled by partnerships with the NFL, the NBA and other top-tier leagues.

“The Star Wars franchise blazed the trail for cinematic fandom and has been the north star of the entertainment industry since the original film debuted in 1977,” said Alexis Scalice, VP, Head of Cosm Studios. “We’re privileged to work alongside our incredible partners to develop an experience that celebrates the film’s legacy and provides fans across generations an opportunity to step inside the beloved Star Wars galaxy like never before.”

Star Wars: A New Hope marks the sixth movie title to play at Cosm, whose roster has spanned the animation, fantasy, horror and sci-fi genres.

Digi International (DGII) Q4 2026 Earnings Call Transcript

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Revenue climbed 24% in fiscal 2026 as the company eyes a pending deal.

Need a quote from a Motley Fool analyst? Email [email protected]

Digi International Inc. (DGII -18.76%) reported record results for fiscal 2026, highlighted by growth across all primary financial metrics and the announcement of a pending $130 million acquisition of Disruptive Technologies. Management indicated that the company is on track to reach its long-term goal of $200 million in annualized recurring revenue in fiscal 2027, one year ahead of the original 2028 target. The strategic focus remains on integrating hardware with subscription-based management services and leveraging the new acquisition to expand the SmartSense solution into European markets. Management noted that the current supply chain environment requires longer-term demand planning and surgical pricing adjustments at the individual SKU level to manage rising component costs, particularly in memory.

We provide them a complete, secure, reliable solution for business and mission-critical applications. We devote about 15% of our revenue to innovation. Innovation spans across hardware, connectivity, software and now increasingly the use and leverage of AI for ROI. We complement this award-winning technology with impeccable service. Our customers need 24/7 expert support. They need flexible solutions, whether they self-manage them or offer Digi to fully manage those solutions. We back that up with a company that's strong, that's thriving, that's executing on its financial and operational priorities. We're happy to announce yet another set of record quarterly revenue, ARR and adjusted EBITDA metrics. And we've delivered results that are above our guidance almost across the Board.

Our ARR, which is the biggest sign of customers accepting Digi solutions is ahead of our target. We combine that financial performance, deploy organic initiatives -- with that cash we generate, we're able to finance acquisitions with debt, pay down the debt through the synergies we are able to capture. For example, recently, we integrated Jolt and Particle into common systems and processes, achieved our financial objectives. We now are on the [ precipice ] of our most recent acquisition, Disruptive Technologies, which extends SmartSense to Europe and [ CSS ] into new markets with new award-winning sensors. So the idea of acquiring, integrating, generating and compounding has now been established within Digi.

The key pillars of our IoT value proposition are a combination of security. And in today's world, security is a rapidly evolving and frequently changing landscape. The advent of AI has brought threats that are now happening on a much more frequent basis, and we are responding with zero trust device authentication and then encryption, automated vulnerability management and having certification to the highest standards of carrier-grade type security. We combine that with 4 decades of reliability, high uptime, longevity of devices that carry you through an average of a 7-year deployment, which is what many of our customers see and really redundant and fault tolerance systems.

Our offering scale both in numbers and across geographies, many of our customers need us to perform across the world. We're managing millions of edge devices. We're cloud native. We're an API-first architecture. Our solutions work as well for small businesses as they do the largest companies in the world. And they've got to be easy to use. They got it easy to provision, intuitive to use and get you really the quickest time to value with plug-and-play hardware and again, complemented by expert support.

And now with the advent of AI, we've got a fifth pillar, which is really leveraging AI, both at the edge and in the cloud to make our systems even more valuable and improving that time to value. We also offer our customers a range of models. As I mentioned earlier, customers can pursue a more traditional model where they purchase our equipment, they use our device management tools, and they really self-manage their solution. They're also increasingly a fully managed solution, where we're offering the complete solution, hardware, software, connectivity services, zero down monthly fee. And quite frankly, for many of our customers, that better matches the ROI.

They don't have a spike of ROI that then tempers over time. They actually build up overall ROI over time, and that better matches a fully managed solutions for many of our customers. In some cases, the customer never owns the equipment and what we're delivering is outcomes. That outcome may be asset uptime. It may be enhanced security, it may be leveraging field resources more efficiently. But they can really leverage the decades of our experience and excellence and decide how much effort they want to put into their IoT solution. We've had success across multiple verticals. We've been a long supplier to infrastructure, utilities and energy.

We're providing connectivity for the smart grid for meters and for renewable solutions where our routers and gateways and [ road ] manager solutions are very popular. We're very, very involved in the healthcare industry, both with medical devices through our Particle by Digi division, but also with SmartSense. We're providing temperature monitoring for sensitive medical applications. In our Opengear division, we provide out-of-band management for both data centers and edge solutions across the globe. We're also involved in retail and point of sale where we're providing really drop-in networking for the use of our digi routers and managed services.

Transportation, we are frequently deployed in a number of mass transit solutions where Digi TX routers are used for visibility of fleet telematics and enabling ridership WiFi, digital signage and other solutions. And lastly, we participate in a number of industrial manufacturing opportunities where our Particle by Digi XBee and ConnectCore solutions provide secure reliability, connectivity for remote access. As I mentioned earlier, we've developed a flywheel over my soon-to-be 12-year tenure. We are using a global database of solutions to source and eventually identify acquisitions that we think can be accretive to the value proposition for our customers and, of course, to our operational model.

We acquired those companies using debt that protects the Digi shareholder and making sure that we're building value that will accrue to the stock price, not just market capitalization. We then integrate those companies, and we take pride in integrating them into our systems, into our culture, into our processes. We then leverage growth opportunities through cross-selling, and we also then look at when appropriate cost base synergies on cost of goods and manufacturing and implement our quality initiatives. We then generate strong cash. That ARR-focused solutions provider really generates cash with a capital-light model that we can then use to repay that debt, lever down and allow us the opportunity to pursue additional acquisitions.

Our recent acquisitions of Jolt and Particle were in the last 2 years. We now have Disruptive Technology that's pending. On Disruptive Technologies, we are joining forces with a company that has, we think, the world's leading IoT sensor. As you can see here, smaller than the postage stamp, incredible versatility in the types of things it can measure and its battery life, which is up to 15 years, ease of deployment and driving ROI in some of the most unexpected places. We are purchasing Disruptive Technologies for $130 million of cash, which we're going to be funding from our credit facility. We expect it to generate $9 million in incremental adjusted EBITDA in our fiscal 2028 period.

It is subject to regulatory approval, which we expect to receive this month. And our forward guidance includes assuming Disruptive Technologies joins Digi on November 30. In calendar '25, you can see Disruptive Technologies had more of a onetime financial model where they sold sensors and had a lower subscription fee. They've deployed over 250,000 sensors. Majority of those are in Europe and many times through channel partners. Digi's model is to flip this on its edge and that recurring revenue will be the focus, and we're going to be embedding those sensors into a subscription rather than having them sold separately onetime. We think that's in the customer's best interest.

It closely matches their outcomes and, of course, helps us build ARR. Why did we choose Disruptive Technologies? As I mentioned, best-in-class sensing. Small sensors, 15-year battery life, a unique protocol, we think is industry-leading and allows us to gain an edge. It also has additional sensing capabilities that we don't offer today or we offer to third parties. We enter the European market. We are taking SmartSense on the road. We're going to leverage the relationships and channel partners that Disruptive Technologies have in place across over 20 European companies. Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang resigns amid spy controversy

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Nvidia CEO and co-founder Jensen Huang has resigned after 33 years leading the company, with an interim replacement taking over immediately.

The announcement on November 12 comes just four days after Nvidia launched an independent investigation into reports that Huang had been targeted by a foreign intelligence operation involving a suspected Chinese spy.

Huang, who co-founded Nvidia in 1993, has stepped down as president, CEO, and a member of the board of directors.

Nvidia confirmed that Colette Kress, its executive vice president and chief financial officer, will serve as interim CEO while the board searches for a permanent replacement.

Kress joined Nvidia in September 2013 and has overseen its financial operations during a period of significant growth. She will retain her existing responsibilities while serving as interim CEO, with the company considering both internal and external candidates for the permanent position.

The leadership change follows reports that Huang had an extramarital relationship with Alice Huang, a former executive at Singapore-based technology company Megaspeed, whom US intelligence officials reportedly suspected of working for Chinese intelligence.

The two are not related, and the allegations prompted Nvidia’s board to announce an independent investigation on November 8, overseen by outside legal counsel.

At the time, Nvidia said it had no reason to believe that national security or company secrets had been compromised. The company also expressed confidence in Huang’s leadership and confirmed that he would cooperate with the investigation.

Huang subsequently acknowledged being unfaithful to his wife in a message to employees, apologizing to his family and colleagues while maintaining his commitment to the company.

The controversy also attracted attention from US lawmakers, with three House committee chairs requesting a closed-door briefing with Huang on November 18 to discuss his interactions with Alice Huang and Nvidia’s security procedures.

Nvidia launches independent investigation into CEO after reports of suspected Chinese spy link

Nvidia stopping RTX 5090 production to focus on AI graphics cards: Report

Nvidia CEO warns US of dangers surrounding Chinese chip war

China’s Foreign Ministry has rejected the espionage allegations, while Singaporean authorities have said they did not identify Alice Huang as a Chinese intelligence asset.

Nvidia’s November 12 announcement did not address the status of its investigation or whether Huang would continue cooperating following his departure.

Alongside Kress’s appointment, Nvidia announced that Harvey C. Jones would become the company’s first-ever chair of the board.

Jones, who has more than 30 years of experience in the technology industry, said the company expects its operations and product roadmap to continue without interruption.

“Colette has helped lead NVIDIA through a period of extraordinary growth and has a deep understanding of our business, customers, operations and financial position,” Jones said.

“She is supported by an experienced leadership team that has been instrumental in executing NVIDIA’s strategy and advancing its technology roadmap. The Board has full confidence in Colette and the leadership team, and we expect the company’s operations, customer commitments and product roadmap to continue without interruption.”

Kress also addressed her appointment, thanking Huang for his contributions to Nvidia over the past three decades.

“NVIDIA’s strength has always come from its people, and together with our leadership team, I am committed to building on the extraordinary foundation Jensen created over the last 33 years,” she said.

“We will continue advancing the work that has made NVIDIA a leader in accelerated computing and AI, and execute on the opportunities ahead. Our focus remains on serving our customers, partners, developers and shareholders.”

Huang’s departure comes as Nvidia faces increased scrutiny over its leadership and business dealings with China, with its advanced AI chips remaining subject to US export restrictions.

The company is scheduled to release its third-quarter fiscal 2027 financial results on November 17, followed by an earnings call later that day. Nvidia confirmed both events will proceed as planned.

Here’s how Google’s Android XR glasses are handling the camera & privacy

📅 2026-11-12  |  Read Source →

Google’s Intelligent Eyewear push with the first Android XR glasses from Warby Parker and Gentle Monster are forced into an unfortunate uphill battle. The “perv glasses” stigma is very real because, frankly, early movers in this category didn’t respect privacy. Here’s how Android XR and Gemini are handling the camera and privacy on “Intelligent Eyewear,” and why I think Google has struck a near-perfect balance.

To quickly summarize the problem at hand (and name names), Meta’s smart glasses developed a reputation of being abused by bad actors who would disable the visible privacy LED (which Meta only recently fixed) or use these glasses in tasteless ways. Combine that with improper handling of recordings on Meta’s part and it’s a recipe for distrust and an earned social stigma. It’s not like Google is a simple bystander here, either. The original Google Glass project was met with the “Glasshole” stigma, and it’s something that Google has clearly learned a lot of lessons from.

It’s a shame, because a camera in smart glasses serves such a functional role, both for the user in just capturing pictures and for the AI assistant built into the experience. I recently talked about how the camera was my favorite part of smart glasses in an issue of our 9to5Google Weekender newsletter, but how it just feels a bit wrong now.

Anyway, with that context in mind, let’s look at how Google is handling this touchy subject.

It starts with the outward-facing light on every part of Android XR glasses, which works similarly to the privacy LED on Meta’s glasses. The key distinction here is this is a “recording light” on Android XR glasses. It turns on whenever the glasses are capturing content that can be viewed later in some form or fashion by the user. So if you’re taking a picture or recording a video, it lights up. If you’re just using Gemini for context, the light doesn’t activate. That’s because any camera captures being used for Gemini are deleted immediately after use. They won’t show up in your Gemini chat logs and that’s not a choice for the user – these images are purged immediately after they’ve been used so that you as the user nor Google can use them. That includes Google not using visuals from Gemini captures for AI training or advertising. Users also have control over what apps on Android XR glasses can use the camera.

Importantly, the light on each pair of Android XR glasses is also designed to avoid any tampering. Android XR glasses automatically perform a system check to ensure the light is working on a regular basis – you might catch the light blinking in white and blue while charging which is a part of the system check ensuring the hardware is fully functional. If the light is found not to be working, the camera is disabled.

One of the pieces of this package that really caught my attention is how Google is handling the privacy of those around you. When your Intelligent Eyewear glasses send a photo to Gemini, your phone automatically redacts faces from the image before it’s sent to Gemini. This happens locally on the phone, essentially as a part of the glasses app. Similarly, voice data sent to Gemini filters out other voices besides your own. The voice filtering bit is just one way in which Google is doing some impressive things with the microphones here, with another example being that the live translation feature works in a 60-degree “cone” in front of you, filtering out other voices in the space.

Continuing on with how the camera interacts with Gemini, Google explained in a conversation with 9to5Google that Gemini is tuned not to use the camera in cases where it’s not obvious that the user wants the camera to be active. An interaction such as “what painting is this” would very clearly imply that the user wants the glasses to view the scene in front of them and identify the art piece, where “how much money is on the table,” Google explained, would ask for a follow-up before using the camera seeing as it’s not obvious that the user expects the camera to be used in that sort of phrase.

Another way in which Android XR glasses will avoid the recording stigma is with their limits. Google is limiting Intelligent Eyewear video recording to 90-second sessions. While that does limit what the user can do, Google said that most recordings fell within that window anyway. Meta, by comparison, offers recordings up to 5 minutes.

Beyond just the glasses app and Gemini, Google says it is making other efforts across its ecosystem to protect against and deter misuse of the camera on Android XR glasses, including:

For the most part, Google’s efforts around privacy on Android XR glasses aren’t groundbreaking. This is how it should work. But seeing as the bar is exceptionally low thanks to a rough start from competitors, I found myself reassured and even a bit impressed by the messaging here – a clear explanation of the indicator light and the automatic on-device face redaction and deletion of Gemini imagery both go a long way.

Google’s sentiment around privacy on glasses is pretty nearly summed up by a sentence in its privacy post today: “…when technology can see the world around you, it has a responsibility to the people around you, too.”
